| - **[President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_South_Korea "President of South Korea")** [Yoon Suk Yeol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoon_Suk_Yeol "Yoon Suk Yeol") (PPP)<br>- [Kim Yong-hyun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Yong-hyun_(general) "Kim Yong-hyun (general)") (MND)<br>- [Park An-su](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Park_An-su "Park An-su") (Martial Law Commander)<br>- [Lee Sang-min](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Sang-min_(lawyer) "Lee Sang-min (lawyer)") (MIS)<br>- [Lee Jin-woo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Jin-woo_(general) "Lee Jin-woo (general)") (Martial Law Commander)<br>- **[Speaker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speaker_of_the_National_Assembly_of_South_Korea "Speaker of the National Assembly of South Korea")** [Woo Won-shik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woo_Won-shik "Woo Won-shik") ( [Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_politician "Independent politician"))<br>- [Lee Jae Myung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Jae_Myung "Lee Jae Myung") (DP)<br>- [Han Dong-hoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_Dong-hoon "Han Dong-hoon") (PPP)<br>- Hon. [Moon Hyung-bae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_Hyung-bae "Moon Hyung-bae") |  |  |

On 3 December 2024, at 22:27 [Korea Standard Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korea_Standard_Time "Korea Standard Time") (KST), [Yoon Suk Yeol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoon_Suk_Yeol "Yoon Suk Yeol"), the then- [president of South Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_South_Korea "President of South Korea"), attempted a [self-coup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-coup "Self-coup") by declaring [martial law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martial_law "Martial law") during a televised address.[&#8202;](#ref-back-theconversation.com-0)[\[3\]](#refnote-theconversation.com) In the address, he accused the [Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_(South_Korea,_2015) "Democratic Party (South Korea, 2015)") (DPK), which held a majority in the [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_(South_Korea) "National Assembly (South Korea)"), of engaging in "anti-state activities" and collaborating with " [North Korean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Korea "North Korea") communists" to undermine the country, describing their dominance as a "legislative dictatorship". The declaration suspended political activities, including sessions of the National Assembly and local legislatures, and [imposed restrictions on the press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_the_press "Freedom of the press"). Yoon ordered the arrest of several political opponents, including leaders of both the DPK and his own [People Power Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_Power_Party_(South_Korea) "People Power Party (South Korea)") (PPP). It was the first declaration of martial law in South Korea since the [military dictatorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coup_d'%C3%A9tat_of_May_Seventeenth "Coup d'état of May Seventeenth") of General [Chun Doo-hwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chun_Doo-hwan "Chun Doo-hwan") in 1980.

The declaration was opposed by both parties and resulted in protests. At 01:02 on 4 December, 190 legislators who had arrived at the [National Assembly Proceeding Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_Proceeding_Hall "National Assembly Proceeding Hall") unanimously passed a motion to lift martial law,[&#8202;](#ref-back-asseminv-0)[\[4\]](#refnote-asseminv) despite attempts by the [Republic of Korea Army Special Warfare Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Korea_Army_Special_Warfare_Command "Republic of Korea Army Special Warfare Command") to prevent the vote. At 04:30, Yoon and his cabinet lifted martial law and soon disbanded the Martial Law Command. The opposition began [impeachment proceedings against Yoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impeachment_of_Yoon_Suk_Yeol "Impeachment of Yoon Suk Yeol") and said it would continue to do so if he did not resign. Uproar over the declaration led to the resignation of several officials in Yoon's administration, including Defense Minister [Kim Yong-hyun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Yong-hyun "Kim Yong-hyun"), who had urged Yoon to enact martial law shortly before the declaration and was second-in-command of the martial law order. Yoon, military officers, and other officials of Yoon's administration were investigated for their role in the implementation of the decree.

On 7 December, Yoon issued an apology for declaring martial law. The next day, the former Defense Minister Kim was arrested and sent to a detention facility for his role in the martial law order, where he [attempted suicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attempt_suicide "Attempt suicide") shortly before a warrant could be filed against him.[&#8202;](#ref-back-YD-0)[\[5\]](#refnote-YD) On 12 December, Yoon said that he would "fight to the end" and that the martial law declaration was an "act of governance" to protect against anti-state forces. It is more widely believed that the declaration was motivated by political issues with the DPK-controlled Assembly over repeated impeachment attempts against officials, opposition to his budget, and various scandals involving him and his wife [Kim Keon Hee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Keon_Hee "Kim Keon Hee").[&#8202;](#ref-back-7-0)[\[6\]](#refnote-7)

Yoon was impeached on 14 December by the National Assembly and suspended from office pending a final ruling by the [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Korea "Constitutional Court of Korea") on whether to confirm his removal from the presidency. Prime Minister [Han Duck-soo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_Duck-soo "Han Duck-soo") served as acting president until [he himself was impeached](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impeachment_of_Han_Duck-soo "Impeachment of Han Duck-soo") on 27 December, making Finance Minister and Deputy Prime Minister [Choi Sang-mok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choi_Sang-mok "Choi Sang-mok") acting president. However, Han's impeachment was overturned by the Constitutional Court on 24 March 2025, reinstating him as acting president. Yoon [was arrested](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrest_of_Yoon_Suk_Yeol "Arrest of Yoon Suk Yeol") on 15 January 2025. On 26 January, he was indicted for leading an [insurrection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insurrection "Insurrection"), becoming the first sitting president to be arrested and indicted in South Korean history. On 4 April, the Constitutional Court unanimously upheld Yoon's impeachment and removal from office over the martial law declaration. On 19 February 2026, Yoon and other figures involved in the incident were found guilty on insurrection charges. Yoon was sentenced to life in prison, while Kim Yong-hyun was sentenced to 30 years in prison.[&#8202;](#ref-back-sentencing1-0)[\[7\]](#refnote-sentencing1)

## Background

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6a/South_Korea_President_Yoon_Suk_Yeol_portrait.jpg/500px-South_Korea_President_Yoon_Suk_Yeol_portrait.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:South_Korea_President_Yoon_Suk_Yeol_portrait.jpg) President [Yoon Suk Yeol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoon_Suk_Yeol "Yoon Suk Yeol") in 2022[![Graph showing the opinion of the presidency Yoon Suk Yeol steadily declining after June 2022 and remaining constant thereafter.](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Opinion_Polling_for_the_Yoon_Suk_Yeol_Presidency.png/500px-Opinion_Polling_for_the_Yoon_Suk_Yeol_Presidency.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Opinion_Polling_for_the_Yoon_Suk_Yeol_Presidency.png) [Opinion polling on the Yoon Suk Yeol presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opinion_polling_on_the_Yoon_Suk_Yeol_presidency "Opinion polling on the Yoon Suk Yeol presidency")

[South Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Korea "South Korea") has been governed as a [presidential democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Republic_of_South_Korea_(1988%E2%80%93present) "Sixth Republic of South Korea (1988–present)") under the [1987 constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_South_Korea "Constitution of South Korea"), which provides for a strong [executive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_(government) "Executive (government)") independent of the legislature. Yoon Suk Yeol, a member of the conservative [People Power Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_Power_Party_(South_Korea) "People Power Party (South Korea)") (PPP) and former [prosecutor general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prosecutor_General_of_South_Korea "Prosecutor General of South Korea"), took office as [President of South Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_South_Korea "President of South Korea") following his victory in the [2022 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_South_Korean_presidential_election "2022 South Korean presidential election").[&#8202;](#ref-back-9-0)[\[8\]](#refnote-9)

Yoon has been criticized for [far-right political views](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far-right_political_views "Far-right political views").[&#8202;](#ref-back-10-0)[\[9\]](#refnote-10)[&#8202;](#ref-back-11-0)[\[10\]](#refnote-11)[&#8202;](#ref-back-12-0)[\[11\]](#refnote-12) His administration had [low approval ratings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opinion_polling_on_the_Yoon_Suk_Yeol_presidency "Opinion polling on the Yoon Suk Yeol presidency"), reaching as low as 17%; in a November 2024 survey, 58% of the population supported either Yoon's resignation or impeachment.[&#8202;](#ref-back-13-0)[\[12\]](#refnote-13) Yoon struggled to achieve his agenda due to opposition from the [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_(South_Korea) "National Assembly (South Korea)"), controlled by the opposition [Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_(South_Korea,_2015) "Democratic Party (South Korea, 2015)") (DPK) since [2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_South_Korean_legislative_election "2020 South Korean legislative election"). In the [April 2024 legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_South_Korean_legislative_election "2024 South Korean legislative election"), the opposition retained its majority, but lacked the two-thirds majority (200 out of 300) required under the constitution to impeach the president.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kim-Tong-Hyung-0)[\[13\]](#refnote-Kim-Tong-Hyung)

Yoon boycotted the opening of the National Assembly, even though it is customary for the president to deliver a speech at the event.[&#8202;](#ref-back-15-0)[\[14\]](#refnote-15) Yoon also opposed investigations into scandals involving his wife [Kim Keon-hee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Keon-hee "Kim Keon-hee") and top officials,[&#8202;](#ref-back-16-0)[\[15\]](#refnote-16) vetoing three separate bills that called for a special counsel investigation into his wife, the third occurring on 26 November 2024.[&#8202;](#ref-back-17-0)[\[16\]](#refnote-17) On 2 December 2024, the opposition-controlled parliament moved to impeach [Board of Audit and Inspection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Board_of_Audit_and_Inspection "Board of Audit and Inspection") Chair Choe Jae-hae and three prosecutors involved in two scandals surrounding Kim,[&#8202;](#ref-back-18-0)[\[17\]](#refnote-18) and rejected the government's 2025 budget proposal.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kim-Sam-0)[\[18\]](#refnote-Kim-Sam)

### Early planning of martial law

During prosecution questioning, former [Defense Counterintelligence Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_Counterintelligence_Command "Defense Counterintelligence Command") (DCC) commander Lieutenant General Yeo In-hyung testified that President Yoon first mentioned "taking emergency action" to address "difficult social issues" in late December 2023, which Yeo interpreted as referring to the martial law declaration. At the end of March 2024, President Yoon invited the then-Defense Minister [Shin Won-sik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shin_Won-sik "Shin Won-sik"), National Intelligence Service Director [Cho Tae-yong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cho_Tae-yong "Cho Tae-yong"), and Presidential Security Service Director [Kim Yong-hyun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Yong-hyun_(general) "Kim Yong-hyun (general)") for dinner and reportedly expressed his intention to "declare martial law soon". At the dinner, Minister Shin and Director Cho expressed their opposition. Minister Shin, concerned about the implementation of martial law, called Director Kim and then-DCC commander Yeo in private right after the dinner to discuss blocking any such moves.[&#8202;](#ref-back-20-0)[\[19\]](#refnote-20) Yeo added that Yoon started mentioning martial law more often following the PPP's defeat in the April 2024 legislative election.[&#8202;](#ref-back-21-0)[\[20\]](#refnote-21) Eventually, Shin was shuffled out of the role of Defense Minister to become Director of the [Office of National Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_National_Security "Office of National Security"), while Kim Yong-hyun was selected to replace Shin in September 2024. The 27 December 2024 unsealed indictment of Kim Yong-hyun revealed that President Yoon met with former Minister Kim, Commander Yeo, and others about 10 times since March 2024 to discuss the imposition of martial law.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kim_Yong-hyun_indictment-0)[\[21\]](#refnote-Kim_Yong-hyun_indictment)

### Warnings of a plot to declare martial law

See also: [Chungam Faction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chungam_Faction "Chungam Faction")

In October 2021, years before the declaration of martial law, Yoon made complimentary remarks about the former [authoritarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarianism "Authoritarianism") [military dictator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_dictator "Military dictator") of South Korea, [Chun Doo-hwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chun_Doo-hwan "Chun Doo-hwan"). The remarks came during a meeting with [People Power Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_Power_Party_(South_Korea) "People Power Party (South Korea)") officials in [Busan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Busan "Busan"), during which Yoon said that "many people still consider Chun as having done well in politics, except the [military coup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coup_d'%C3%A9tat_of_December_Twelfth "Coup d'état of December Twelfth") and the [Gwangju Uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwangju_Uprising "Gwangju Uprising")", later adding that he believed even people in [Honam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honam "Honam"), the geographic area including [Gwangju](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwangju "Gwangju"), felt the same way.[&#8202;](#ref-back-23-0)[\[22\]](#refnote-23)

After Yoon was elected president, when asked about the possibility of pressure and protests by opposition parties and citizens, Defense Minister Kim Yong-hyun responded: "Why worry about it? Just declare martial law and sweep it all away."[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kimfacts3-0)[\[23\]](#refnote-Kimfacts3) Yoon began appointing his fellow [Chungam High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chungam_High_School "Chungam High School") alumni as high-ranking figures in his administration and the military, a group referred to as the [Chungam Faction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chungam_Faction "Chungam Faction"). Throughout his political career, several of Defense Minister Kim's former high school alumni said that his favorite book to read was [Adolf Hitler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adolf_Hitler "Adolf Hitler")'s autobiographical manifesto _[Mein Kampf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mein_Kampf "Mein Kampf")_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-25-0)[\[24\]](#refnote-25) DPK Representative [Choo Mi-ae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choo_Mi-ae "Choo Mi-ae") confirmed this during a televised interview, which added to many people's suspicion that Kim, having risen to such a powerful position within both the military and the government, may have been driven by darker instincts rather than a sense of duty to national security.[&#8202;](#ref-back-KimMeinKampf-0)[\[25\]](#refnote-KimMeinKampf)

In September 2024, three months before the martial law declaration, some DPK politicians began suggesting that Yoon was preparing martial law. Party leader [Lee Jae Myung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Jae_Myung "Lee Jae Myung") alluded to "speculation about martial law preparations", saying that Yoon appointing Kim Yong-hyun as Defense Minister was a part of a martial law strategy to prevent Kim Keon-hee from going to prison.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Um-0)[\[26\]](#refnote-Um) [Kim Min-seok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Min-seok_(politician) "Kim Min-seok (politician)"), member of the party's Supreme Council, stated: "I have well-founded reasons to believe that the [conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatism_in_South_Korea "Conservatism in South Korea") Yoon administration is drawing up a contingency plan to declare martial law."[&#8202;](#ref-back-28-0)[\[27\]](#refnote-28)[&#8202;](#ref-back-29-0)[\[28\]](#refnote-29) The Presidential Office dismissed this claim as "groundless".[&#8202;](#ref-back-30-0)[\[29\]](#refnote-30) People Power Party floor leader [Choo Kyung-ho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choo_Kyung-ho "Choo Kyung-ho") also denied the possibility of martial law, saying "Such theories ... are no more than scare tactics and propaganda based purely on imagination".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Um-1)[\[26\]](#refnote-Um)

Kim Min-seok justified his prediction of a martial law declaration by Yoon by adding that he was "one of the people who obtained information and reported that the government was preparing to invoke martial law during the [impeachment of President Park Geun-hye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impeachment_of_Park_Geun-hye "Impeachment of Park Geun-hye")" in 2016, despite denials by the Park government at the time. A document was produced in secret by the [Defense Security Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_Counterintelligence_Command "Defense Counterintelligence Command") (now the Defense Counterintelligence Command) in February 2017 that considered invoking martial law in anticipation of continued street protests if Park Geun-hye was not removed by the Constitutional Court. The 67-page document titled "Wartime Martial Law and Joint Action Plan"[&#8202;](#ref-back-31-0)[\[30\]](#refnote-31) contained specific plans including mobilizing tanks to suppress [candlelight protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candlelight_vigil "Candlelight vigil") in [Gwanghwamun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwanghwamun_Square "Gwanghwamun Square") in the event that the impeachment of Park was dismissed, and arresting and detaining lawmakers to prevent the National Assembly from reaching the quorum required to lift martial law if lawmakers attempted to do so (similar to the martial law plans of 2024).[&#8202;](#ref-back-32-0)[\[31\]](#refnote-32)

Several South Korean Defense Ministers have had major roles in either declarations of martial law or self-coups in the past, with the most notable examples being [Song Yo-chan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Song_Yo-chan "Song Yo-chan"), who declared martial law and demanded the resignation of President [Syngman Rhee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syngman_Rhee "Syngman Rhee"); and [Chung Ho-yong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chung_Ho-yong "Chung Ho-yong"), who had complicity in the [Gwangju Uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwangju_Uprising "Gwangju Uprising"). During his confirmation hearing for Minister of National Defense at the National Assembly on 2 September 2024, Kim Yong-hyun was asked by members of the opposition as to whether he would declare martial law or recommend Yoon to do so. He replied: "I think talk about martial law is out of date; if it is declared, who would accept it? Do you think the military would even follow the order?" Kim was also questioned on his close personal ties with Yoon, as the two have known each other since high school. Kim took office as Minister of National Defense on 6 September. Over the following three months, he allegedly planned military rule under martial law.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Jung-0)[\[32\]](#refnote-Jung)

Following the botched martial law implementation, Kim Min-seok cited the Yoon administration's usage of the term "anti-state forces", and the existence of the Chungam Faction as indications of Yoon's ulterior motive for martial law. He added that Yoon's government "is so bad at governance that they have no way of preventing their replacement other than martial law, terrorism, and mobilizing the legal system against their opponents".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kim_Min_seok_Hankyoreh-0)[\[33\]](#refnote-Kim_Min_seok_Hankyoreh) Documents obtained by Choo Mi-ae showed that the DCC had prepared documents and other related material on declaring martial law following orders from its commander, Lieutenant General Yeo In-hyung, in November 2024.[&#8202;](#ref-back-35-0)[\[34\]](#refnote-35) Police investigators also found that Defense Intelligence Command chief Major General Moon Sang-ho, his predecessor, Roh Sang-won and former military police commander of the [3rd Field Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3rd_Army_(South_Korea) "3rd Army (South Korea)") Command Kim Yong-gun also met at a [Lotteria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lotteria "Lotteria") fast food restaurant in [Ansan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ansan "Ansan") on 1 December to discuss preparations for the declaration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-36-0)[\[35\]](#refnote-36) The Chungam Faction whom Yoon and Kim appointed to high ranking positions and cited as having a role in the martial law declaration were commander of the [Defense Counterintelligence Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_Counterintelligence_Command "Defense Counterintelligence Command") [Yeo In-hyung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeo_In-hyung "Yeo In-hyung"), Chief of the [Army Special Warfare Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Korea_Army_Special_Warfare_Command "Republic of Korea Army Special Warfare Command") [Kwak Jong-geun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kwak_Jong-geun "Kwak Jong-geun"), commander of the Defense Security Agency of the [Defense Intelligence Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_Intelligence_Agency_(South_Korea) "Defense Intelligence Agency (South Korea)") Park Jong-seon, commander of the Capital Defense Command [Lee Jin-woo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Jin-woo_(general) "Lee Jin-woo (general)"), Minister of the Interior and Safety [Lee Sang-min](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Sang-min_(lawyer) "Lee Sang-min (lawyer)"), director of the Planning and Management Office of the Counterintelligence Command Park Sung-ha, and Chief of the 101st Security Brigade Hwang Se-young.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kim_Min_seok_Hankyoreh-1)[\[33\]](#refnote-Kim_Min_seok_Hankyoreh)[&#8202;](#ref-back-37-0)[\[36\]](#refnote-37)[&#8202;](#ref-back-January2025-0)[\[37\]](#refnote-January2025)

Yoon and Kim met each other while attending Chungam High School. Kim also allegedly knew Yeo In-hyung subsequent to his graduation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-yeo-0)[\[38\]](#refnote-yeo) Since the other members of the faction graduated years apart from both Yoon and Kim, they did not know them personally. It is alleged that Yoon met them sometime during his presidential campaign.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Alumni-0)[\[39\]](#refnote-Alumni) The Chungam Faction has been compared to the [Hanahoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanahoe "Hanahoe") faction of former dictator [Chun Doo-hwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chun_Doo-hwan "Chun Doo-hwan") and his successor [Roh Tae-woo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roh_Tae-woo "Roh Tae-woo").[&#8202;](#ref-back-41-0)[\[40\]](#refnote-41)

### Attempts to provoke North Korean crisis to justify martial law

See also: [2024 South Korean drone intrusion incident in North Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_South_Korean_drone_intrusion_incident_in_North_Korea "2024 South Korean drone intrusion incident in North Korea")

Defense Minister [Kim Yong-hyun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Yong-hyun "Kim Yong-hyun") at a joint meeting at [The Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon "The Pentagon") with U.S. Secretary of Defense [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin "Lloyd Austin") regarding North Korea relations on 30 October 2024

On 1 December, two days before martial law was declared, colonel-level commanders were called to be on guard for an imminent provocation by North Korea, with DCC Commander Yeo In-hyung giving orders to prepare for a "serious North Korean filth balloon situation".[&#8202;](#ref-back-42-0)[\[41\]](#refnote-42) During police investigations into the martial law declaration, a memo was recovered from a notebook owned by former Army Maj. Gen. Roh Sang-won, former chief of the Defense Intelligence Command, in his residence in [Ansan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ansan "Ansan"). The memo reportedly contained details of a plot to provoke North Korea by causing an incident along the [Northern Limit Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Limit_Line "Northern Limit Line").[&#8202;](#ref-back-43-0)[\[42\]](#refnote-43) On 14 February 2025, the _[Hankyoreh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hankyoreh "The Hankyoreh")_ reported that the notebook included, in addition to the North Korean plans, plans for detaining about 500 public figures and abolishing term limits to allow Yoon to potentially serve 3 terms.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Hankyoreh_-_Noh_Sang-won_notebook-0)[\[43\]](#refnote-Hankyoreh_-_Noh_Sang-won_notebook) On 21 July 2025, Maj. Gen. Kim Yong-dae, head of the Drone Operations Command, was suspended from duty over suspicions that military drones were illegally dispatched to North Korea as part of preparations for Yoon's imposition of martial law.[&#8202;](#ref-back-45-0)[\[44\]](#refnote-45)

### Martial law powers and past occurrences

Martial law was a recurring tool used by military leaders to suppress dissent and consolidate power in South Korea's authoritarian, dictatorial past until the end of the [Fifth Republic of Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifth_Republic_of_Korea "Fifth Republic of Korea") with the [June Democratic Struggle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_Democratic_Struggle "June Democratic Struggle") of 1987, when South Korea transitioned to democracy under the Sixth Republic. This was the first time martial law had been declared in South Korea since the [1980 military coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coup_d'%C3%A9tat_of_May_Seventeenth "Coup d'état of May Seventeenth") after [the assassination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Park_Chung_Hee "Assassination of Park Chung Hee") of dictator [Park Chung Hee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Park_Chung_Hee "Park Chung Hee"), and the restoration of civil liberties in 1987.[&#8202;](#ref-back-46-0)[\[45\]](#refnote-46) This was the 17th since the [foundation of the republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Republic_of_Korea "First Republic of Korea") in 1948.[&#8202;](#ref-back-YD-1)[\[5\]](#refnote-YD)

According to the Martial Law Act, the declaration of martial law or alteration in its nature by the President shall undergo deliberation by the State Council. The Minister of National Defense or the Minister of the Interior and Safety may suggest the declaration of martial law to the President through the Prime Minister.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Statutes-0)[\[46\]](#refnote-Statutes) Under [Article 77](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Constitution%20of%20the%20Republic%20of%20Korea%20(1987)#Article_77 "wikisource:Constitution of the Republic of Korea (1987)") of the constitution, the president must immediately notify the National Assembly when declaring martial law. This enables special measures to be taken, including reducing the need for arrest warrants and restricting freedom of speech, the press, assembly, and association. The National Assembly can lift martial law by a simple majority vote.[&#8202;](#ref-back-48-0)[\[47\]](#refnote-48)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Foreign_Policy_magazine_self-coup-0)[\[48\]](#refnote-Foreign_Policy_magazine_self-coup) The president has no authority to suspend the Assembly by any means, and "during the enforcement of martial law, no member of the Assembly shall be arrested or detained unless he/she is caught [in the act of committing a crime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In_flagrante_delicto "In flagrante delicto")".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Statutes-1)[\[46\]](#refnote-Statutes)

## Events

### Events on 3 December before the martial law declaration

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/CC-TV_still_shows_Numerous_of_Yoon_Suk_Yeol%27s_Cabinet_Members_inside_the_Presidential_Office_the_Day_of_the_2024_Martial_Law_Declaration.png/500px-CC-TV_still_shows_Numerous_of_Yoon_Suk_Yeol%27s_Cabinet_Members_inside_the_Presidential_Office_the_Day_of_the_2024_Martial_Law_Declaration.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:CC-TV_still_shows_Numerous_of_Yoon_Suk_Yeol's_Cabinet_Members_inside_the_Presidential_Office_the_Day_of_the_2024_Martial_Law_Declaration.png) CCTV still showing numerous of President Yoon's cabinet members inside the Presidential Office shortly before the declaration. Defense Minister Kim is standing and gesturing how many more cabinet members are needed for a quorum for the meeting

On the morning of 3 December, former Army Maj. Gen. Roh Sang-won, former chief of the Defense Intelligence Command met with Defense Minister Kim at his official residence, for a meeting lasting 20 to 30 minutes. In the afternoon, Minister Kim instructed Army Chief of Staff, Gen. Park An-su, and five other military officials to report to his office at 21:30.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Joongang_timeline_from_testimonies-0)[\[49\]](#refnote-Joongang_timeline_from_testimonies)

At 17:00 [KST](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korea_Standard_Time "Korea Standard Time"), units of the [Republic of Korea Army Special Warfare Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Korea_Army_Special_Warfare_Command "Republic of Korea Army Special Warfare Command"), including the [707th Special Mission Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/707th_Special_Mission_Group "707th Special Mission Group"), [1st Special Forces Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1st_Special_Forces_Brigade_(South_Korea) "1st Special Forces Brigade (South Korea)"), and the [13th Special Mission Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/13th_Special_Mission_Brigade_(South_Korea) "13th Special Mission Brigade (South Korea)") received orders to prepare for operations at an isolated area. The 707th SMG received a message to prepare for the conduct of a real-world operation by helicopter, and deployment on orders of Defense Minister Kim. They were told "the situation related to North Korea is serious", though an anonymous official reported there had been no movements by the North Korean military.[&#8202;](#ref-back-51-0)[\[50\]](#refnote-51)[&#8202;](#ref-back-KoreaPro-0)[\[51\]](#refnote-KoreaPro)

At 18:20, [Korean National Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Police_Agency_(South_Korea) "National Police Agency (South Korea)") Commissioner Cho Ji-ho reportedly received an order from the Presidential Office to "be on standby". At a subsequent committee inquiry by the National Assembly, Cho claimed he did not know the martial law plan until its announcement.[&#8202;](#ref-back-53-0)[\[52\]](#refnote-53) However, subsequent investigations revealed that Cho, and Seoul Metropolitan Police Agency Commissioner General Kim Bong-sik met with Minister Kim and President Yoon at 19:00, and the two police officials were given a list of about 10 people to be arrested along with plans to occupy the National Assembly and National Election Commission via a martial law declaration at 22:00.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Joongang_timeline_from_testimonies-1)[\[49\]](#refnote-Joongang_timeline_from_testimonies)

At 21:50, broadcasting networks received a message: "There will be an emergency government announcement, please connect to the live broadcast". Journalists covering the Presidential Office were barred from entering the briefing room, where such broadcasts are normally made.[&#8202;](#ref-back-54-0)[\[53\]](#refnote-54) Just before declaring martial law, Yoon reportedly told his cabinet members: "Even my wife doesn't know about this. Otherwise, she'd be furious."[&#8202;](#ref-back-heonheeinvestigate-0)[\[54\]](#refnote-heonheeinvestigate)

### Declaration of martial law

|  |
| --- |
| **3 December ( [KST](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_Standard_Time "Korean Standard Time"))**<br>- 22:23: Yoon Suk Yeol makes his emergency address to the nation.<br>- 22:27: Yoon Suk Yeol declares martial law.<br>- 22:42: The opposition Democratic Party, which holds a legislative majority, urgently convenes National Assembly members.<br>- 23:04: The National Assembly entrance is closed.<br>- 23:25: Army Chief of Staff [Park An-su](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Park_An-su "Park An-su") is appointed martial law commander.<br>- 23:30: Park An-su declares decree on martial law.<br>- 23:45: Defense Minister Kim Yong-hyun orders the entire military to be on high alert.<br>- Around 23:57: The [707th Special Mission Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/707th_Special_Mission_Group "707th Special Mission Group") of the Republic of Korea Armed Forces appear in front of the [National Assembly Proceeding Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_Proceeding_Hall "National Assembly Proceeding Hall"), and clashes with aides and congressional staff continue. |
| **4 December ( [KST](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_Standard_Time "Korean Standard Time"))**<br>- 00:12: Martial law troops attempt to enter the back door of the National Assembly.<br>- 00:36: Airborne troops break through the glass windows of the National Assembly and enter.<br>- 00:48: The National Assembly commence an emergency session to vote to end martial law.<br>- 00:50: Yoon Suk Yeol and Kim Yong-hyun order Commander Kwak Jong-geun and Capital Defense Commander Lee Jin-woo to break through the plenary chamber doors to drag the lawmakers out.<br>- 01:02: The National Assembly votes unanimously to lift martial law.<br>- 01:08: [Woo Won-shik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woo_Won-shik "Woo Won-shik"), [Speaker of the National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speaker_of_the_National_Assembly_of_South_Korea "Speaker of the National Assembly of South Korea"), tells the military to leave the National Assembly Proceeding Hall.<br>- 01:11: Martial law forces inside and outside the National Assembly begin to withdraw.<br>- 04:26: Yoon Suk Yeol accepts the National Assembly's vote, withdraws the military, and announces in a public address that martial law would be lifted.<br>- 04:30: Yoon Suk Yeol's cabinet approves a motion to terminate martial law.<br>- 04:49: The Martial Law Command is disbanded. |
| - [![Wikisource logo](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Wikisource-logo.svg/40px-Wikisource-logo.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Wikisource-logo.svg) The full text of [Translation:2024 Declaration of Martial Law in the Republic of Korea](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Translation:2024%20Declaration%20of%20Martial%20Law%20in%20the%20Republic%20of%20Korea "wikisource:Translation:2024 Declaration of Martial Law in the Republic of Korea") at Wikisource<br>- [![Wikisource logo](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Wikisource-logo.svg/40px-Wikisource-logo.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Wikisource-logo.svg) The full text of [2024년 대한민국 비상계엄 선포](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/ko:2024%EB%85%84%20%EB%8C%80%ED%95%9C%EB%AF%BC%EA%B5%AD%20%EB%B9%84%EC%83%81%EA%B3%84%EC%97%84%20%EC%84%A0%ED%8F%AC "wikisource:ko:2024년 대한민국 비상계엄 선포") at Wikisource |

2024 South Korean martial law timeline

At 22:23 on 3 December 2024, Yoon Suk Yeol began making his emergency address to the nation, and at 22:27, declared martial law. He accused the opposition of being an "anti-state ... den of criminals"[&#8202;](#ref-back-56-0)[\[55\]](#refnote-56) and "trying to overthrow the free democracy" by impeaching members of [his cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_Yoon_Suk_Yeol "Cabinet of Yoon Suk Yeol") and blocking his budget plans. He asked citizens to believe in him and tolerate "some inconveniences",[&#8202;](#ref-back-57-0)[\[56\]](#refnote-57)[&#8202;](#ref-back-58-0)[\[57\]](#refnote-58) and also said that there was a North Korean conspiracy against the South Korean government.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Sky_News-0)[\[58\]](#refnote-Sky_News)

Prime Minister [Han Duck-soo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_Duck-soo "Han Duck-soo") was allegedly sidelined in the decision-making process leading up to the martial law declaration, and discussions were kept private between Yoon and Defense Minister Kim Yong-hyun.[&#8202;](#ref-back-KTimes-overlook-0)[\[59\]](#refnote-KTimes-overlook)[&#8202;](#ref-back-61-0)[\[60\]](#refnote-61) Kim urged Yoon to enact martial law during a cabinet meeting[&#8202;](#ref-back-62-0)[\[61\]](#refnote-62)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Ye-0)[\[62\]](#refnote-Ye) shortly before the official declaration that ran from 22:17 to 22:22,[&#8202;](#ref-back-64-0)[\[63\]](#refnote-64) in which a majority of the 19 cabinet members were "strongly against" the decision.[&#8202;](#ref-back-65-0)[\[64\]](#refnote-65) Yoon also ordered finance minister [Choi Sang-mok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choi_Sang-mok "Choi Sang-mok") to draft a budget for an emergency legislative body which was to have been created during the martial law declaration[&#8202;](#ref-back-66-0)[\[65\]](#refnote-66) and ordered interior minister [Lee Sang-min](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Sang-min_(lawyer) "Lee Sang-min (lawyer)") to cut off electricity and water supplies to media outlets critical of Yoon, specifically the _[Hankyoreh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hankyoreh "Hankyoreh")_, the _[Kyunghyang Shinmun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyunghyang_Shinmun "Kyunghyang Shinmun")_, [MBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munhwa_Broadcasting_Corporation "Munhwa Broadcasting Corporation") and [JTBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JTBC "JTBC"), as well as the opinion polling agency Flower Research.[&#8202;](#ref-back-67-0)[\[66\]](#refnote-67)

At 22:30, Defense Minister Kim addressed a video conference giving orders to all military commanders above the corps commander level and threatened officers with punishment for insubordination if they did not follow the orders.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Joongang_timeline_from_testimonies-2)[\[49\]](#refnote-Joongang_timeline_from_testimonies) Yoon appointed [Chief of Staff of the Republic of Korea Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_Staff_of_the_Republic_of_Korea_Army "Chief of Staff of the Republic of Korea Army") [Park An-su](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Park_An-su "Park An-su") as his martial law commander.[&#8202;](#ref-back-68-0)[\[67\]](#refnote-68) A defense ministry report obtained by DPK representative Bak Seung-a said that around 1,580 troops, 107 military vehicles, 12 [Black Hawk helicopters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Hawk_helicopter "Black Hawk helicopter") and more than 9,000 rounds of live ammunition were deployed for the implementation of martial law.[&#8202;](#ref-back-69-0)[\[68\]](#refnote-69) [MBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munhwa_Broadcasting_Corporation "Munhwa Broadcasting Corporation") reported that the military brought 4,980 blank ammunition rounds and 100 cartridges for stun guns throughout its mobilization for martial law, while 5,000 live ammunition rounds and some 3,000 blanks were deployed at the National Assembly alone.[&#8202;](#ref-back-70-0)[\[69\]](#refnote-70)

On 26 May 2025, Choi Sang-mok, Han Duck-soo and Lee Sang-min were summoned for questioning by police.[&#8202;](#ref-back-71-0)[\[70\]](#refnote-71) A day later, media reported that both Choi Sang-mok and Han Duck-soo were slapped with a travel ban by police, as further investigations and analysis of CCTV footage found inconsistencies between the footage and prior statements made by the three former officials over their involvement in the martial law declaration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-72-0)[\[71\]](#refnote-72) CCTV footage obtained from the Presidential Office showed Prime Minister Han receiving and reviewing martial law documents just before Yoon declaring martial law in the reception room on the second floor of the President's office, thus showing that Han gave false testimony when he claimed that he had no foreknowledge.[&#8202;](#ref-back-73-0)[\[72\]](#refnote-73)

Following the beginning of the special counsel investigation appointed in June 2025 by new president [Lee Jae Myung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Jae_Myung "Lee Jae Myung"), it was reported on 1 July by the Hankyoreh that after Yoon declared martial law, his deputy chief of staff Kang Eui-gu belatedly drafted a new martial law declaration to be signed by the prime minister and defense minister. Reportedly, Yoon and his advisers had realized the illegality of the martial law declaration and attempted to correct that retroactively, pursuant to Article 82 of the Constitution, which states: "The acts of the President under law shall be executed in writing, and such documents shall be countersigned by the Prime Minister and the members of the State Council concerned." Yoon had already failed to abide to this by failing to prepare written versions of his martial law declaration or notification to the National Assembly. Kang called Prime Minister Han with the plan of retroactively drafting a martial law declaration, with the signatures of Han, Yoon and Defense Minister Kim. Han signed this document, but later asked for it to be destroyed because a retroactive document could provoke more controversy if it became known.[&#8202;](#ref-back-74-0)[\[73\]](#refnote-74) Kang Eui-gu was also believed to have forged minutes of Cabinet meeting to make it look like it lasted for 40 minutes, instead of the actual 5 minutes that took place.[&#8202;](#ref-back-75-0)[\[74\]](#refnote-75)

### Election Commission raids

[![Soldiers accessing Election Commission HQ servers](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Martial_law_troops_election_commission.jpg/500px-Martial_law_troops_election_commission.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Martial_law_troops_election_commission.jpg) CCTV images of soldiers in the National Election Commission headquarters server room.

After martial law was declared, armed troops raided the [National Election Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Election_Commission_(South_Korea) "National Election Commission (South Korea)") (NEC) headquarters in [Gwacheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwacheon "Gwacheon"), [Gyeonggi Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gyeonggi_Province "Gyeonggi Province") (10 troops), one of its training centers in [Suwon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suwon "Suwon"), Gyeonggi Province (130 troops) and the National Election Survey Deliberation Commission in [Gwanak District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwanak_District "Gwanak District") in southern Seoul (47 troops).[&#8202;](#ref-back-76-0)[\[75\]](#refnote-76) NEC officials allegedly had their phones confiscated.[&#8202;](#ref-back-77-0)[\[76\]](#refnote-77)

Several operatives from the Intelligence Protection Group of the DCC[&#8202;](#ref-back-78-0)[\[77\]](#refnote-78)\[ _[full citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources#What_information_to_include "Wikipedia:Citing sources")_\] with firearms and uniforms that were missing unit insignias and patches[&#8202;](#ref-back-79-0)[\[78\]](#refnote-79) raided the server room located on the second floor of the NEC headquarters[&#8202;](#ref-back-80-0)[\[79\]](#refnote-80)[&#8202;](#ref-back-81-0)[\[80\]](#refnote-81) while the Special Warfare Command troops[&#8202;](#ref-back-82-0)[\[81\]](#refnote-82) and National Police officers from Gyeonggi Nambu Provincial Police Agency provided perimeter security at the building. Lieutenant General Yeo In-hyung, head of the DCC and another member of the Chungam faction, was behind the raid.[&#8202;](#ref-back-83-0)[\[82\]](#refnote-83) Commander Yeo made a call to KNP Commissioner Cho Ji-ho about the NEC headquarters raid, and Cho started supplying police forces for the troops.[&#8202;](#ref-back-84-0)[\[83\]](#refnote-84)

Kim Yong-hyun said he deployed troops to determine the need for an investigation into the NEC's alleged "election fraud" in the April 2024 National Assembly election, a claim echoed by far-right [YouTubers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouTuber "YouTuber").[&#8202;](#ref-back-85-0)[\[84\]](#refnote-85) These allegations have been repeatedly debunked by authorities.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Korea_Times_election_fraud-0)[\[85\]](#refnote-Korea_Times_election_fraud) Representative Lee Jun-seok of the Reform Party, said that President Yoon repeatedly talked about election fraud while Lee was meeting him in his capacity as the leader of Yoon's PPP party, accusing Yoon of "being crazy" about conspiracy theories, and alleged that Yoon was surrounded by individuals who believed in conspiracy theories.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Korea_Times_election_fraud-1)[\[85\]](#refnote-Korea_Times_election_fraud)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Korea_Times_election_fraud_2-0)[\[86\]](#refnote-Korea_Times_election_fraud_2)

DCC martial law troops assigned to raid the NEC training center in Suwon questioned their deployment orders, and delayed their arrival by slowing their pace or pulling off at rest stops.[&#8202;](#ref-back-88-0)[\[87\]](#refnote-88) On 9 December, Representative Lee released a report stating that a DCC [brigadier general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigadier_general "Brigadier general") assaulted a [major](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_(rank) "Major (rank)") for questioning deployment orders and forced him on a bus to seize the NEC servers. Representative Lee said that some troops wasted time after arriving at the NEC by eating [ramyeon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramyeon "Ramyeon") at a nearby convenience store, after judging their deployment orders to be illegal.[&#8202;](#ref-back-89-0)[\[88\]](#refnote-89) The brigadier general was identified as Kim Dae-woo, a close associate of DCC Commander Yeo.[&#8202;](#ref-back-90-0)[\[89\]](#refnote-90)[&#8202;](#ref-back-91-0)[\[90\]](#refnote-91)[&#8202;](#ref-back-92-0)[\[91\]](#refnote-92)

On 12 December, Yoon alleged in a national address that the NEC's network system was vulnerable to cyberattacks and hacking, resulting in election fraud. The president accused the NEC of invoking constitutional immunity as an excuse to avoid inspection of its internal system in 2023 and claimed that the National Intelligence Service had discovered a North Korean hacking attack on the system.[&#8202;](#ref-back-NEC_election_fraud-0)[\[92\]](#refnote-NEC_election_fraud) The NEC released a statement calling Yoon's allegation "baseless" and said Yoon's "raising suspicions of electoral fraud ... amounts to self-denial of the electoral management system through which he was elected president".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Korea_Times_election_fraud_2-1)[\[86\]](#refnote-Korea_Times_election_fraud_2)[&#8202;](#ref-back-94-0)[\[93\]](#refnote-94) The statement added that election fraud would require organized action from NEC aides, a breakdown of the security management system, and switching out identical amounts of actual voting papers, and called it "almost impossible".[&#8202;](#ref-back-NEC_election_fraud-1)[\[92\]](#refnote-NEC_election_fraud) On 13 December, NEC Chairman Rho Tae-ak said in a National Assembly hearing that sending martial-law troops to take over the NEC was inexcusably illegal and unconstitutional.[&#8202;](#ref-back-95-0)[\[94\]](#refnote-95) On 19 December, the NIS confirmed, in a report to the National Assembly, that there was no evidence of election rigging.[&#8202;](#ref-back-96-0)[\[95\]](#refnote-96)

The unsealed indictment of former Minister Kim on 27 December revealed that Commander of the Intelligence Command Moon Sang-ho and his predecessor Roh Sang-won intended for 30 troops to detain NEC staff, tie them with ropes, put masks on their faces, and send them to the Water Defense Command Bunker for election fraud. Evidence was found of awls, ropes, hammers, cable ties and baseball bats belonging to the arrest team to be used when carrying out the arrests.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kim_Yong-hyun_indictment-1)[\[21\]](#refnote-Kim_Yong-hyun_indictment)

### Plans for mass detention of political and public figures

At 22:28, Defense Minister Kim instructed DCC Commander Lieutenant General Yeo In-hyung to arrest several politicians on a target list.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Joongang_timeline_from_testimonies-3)[\[49\]](#refnote-Joongang_timeline_from_testimonies) According to testimony by [National Intelligence Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Intelligence_Service_(South_Korea) "National Intelligence Service (South Korea)") (NIS) Deputy Director [Hong Jang-won](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Jang-won "Hong Jang-won") to the National Assembly on 6 December, Yoon called him at 22:53 on 3 December and ordered him to help the DCC arrest his political opponents, and that Yoon wanted to "use this chance to arrest them and wipe them out".[&#8202;](#ref-back-BBC-0)[\[96\]](#refnote-BBC)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Chosun_Ilbo_-_arrest_list-0)[\[97\]](#refnote-Chosun_Ilbo_-_arrest_list)[&#8202;](#ref-back-99-0)[\[98\]](#refnote-99)[&#8202;](#ref-back-100-0)[\[99\]](#refnote-100) Hong testified that Yeo gave him a list of individuals targeted for arrest which included Democratic Party leader [Lee Jae Myung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Jae_Myung "Lee Jae Myung"), National Assembly Speaker [Woo Won-shik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woo_Won-shik "Woo Won-shik"), People Power Party leader [Han Dong-hoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_Dong-hoon "Han Dong-hoon"), Democratic Party Representative [Kim Min-seok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Min-seok_(politician) "Kim Min-seok (politician)"), Democratic Party floor leader [Park Chan-dae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Park_Chan-dae_(politician) "Park Chan-dae (politician)"), National Assembly Legislation and Judiciary Committee Chair [Jung Chung-rae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jung_Chung-rae "Jung Chung-rae"), [Rebuilding Korea Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebuilding_Korea_Party "Rebuilding Korea Party") leader [Cho Kuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cho_Kuk "Cho Kuk"), journalist [Kim Ou-joon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Ou-joon "Kim Ou-joon"), former Supreme Court Chief Justice [Kim Myeong-su](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Myeong-su "Kim Myeong-su"), leader of the [Candlelight Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_candlelight_rallies_in_South_Korea "History of candlelight rallies in South Korea") [Kim Min-woong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Min-woong?action=edit&redlink=1 "Kim Min-woong")\[ [ko](https://ko.wikipedia.org/wiki/%EA%B9%80%EB%AF%BC%EC%9B%85 "ko:김민웅")\], and former National Election Commission (NEC) Chairman [Kwon Soon-il](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kwon_Soon-il?action=edit&redlink=1 "Kwon Soon-il")\[ [ko](https://ko.wikipedia.org/wiki/%EA%B6%8C%EC%88%9C%EC%9D%BC%20(%EB%B2%95%EC%A1%B0%EC%9D%B8) "ko:권순일 (법조인)")\].[&#8202;](#ref-back-Chosun_Ilbo_-_arrest_list-1)[\[97\]](#refnote-Chosun_Ilbo_-_arrest_list)

Hong said that there were others on the list, including another NEC member whose name he could not recall and the head of a major labor union, either the [Federation of Korean Trade Unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federation_of_Korean_Trade_Unions "Federation of Korean Trade Unions") or the [Korean Confederation of Trade Unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_Confederation_of_Trade_Unions "Korean Confederation of Trade Unions"). Han Dong-hoon, the leader of Yoon's own People Power Party, confirmed that he was on the list of targets and that Yoon had planned to jail arrested politicians in a detention center in Gwacheon, south of Seoul.[&#8202;](#ref-back-bbc6dec-0)[\[100\]](#refnote-bbc6dec) Upon receiving Yeo's instructions, Hong said he thought Yoon had lost his mind. He refused to comply, citing the NIS's lack of resources and means to carry out such an order. Yeo outlined plans to arrest first and second-tier targets in phases, detain them at DCC facilities, and conduct investigations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Chosun_Ilbo_-_arrest_list-2)[\[97\]](#refnote-Chosun_Ilbo_-_arrest_list) A bunker designated as an operations center for potential North Korean attacks and a military police compound, both operated by the Capital Defense Command in Seoul's Gwanak District, were also considered as detention sites.[&#8202;](#ref-back-102-0)[\[101\]](#refnote-102)

Yoon then fired Hong. The report alleged Hong was given the orders because NIS Director [Cho Tae-yong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cho_Tae-yong "Cho Tae-yong") had previously refused to act on Yoon's arrest list.[&#8202;](#ref-back-103-0)[\[102\]](#refnote-103) Troops were deployed in front of the building where Kim Ou-joon, a [YouTuber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouTuber "YouTuber") and liberal journalist on the list of targets, created his videos.[&#8202;](#ref-back-104-0)[\[103\]](#refnote-104) Kim said that soldiers were also deployed at his residence and posted footage showing about 20 armed soldiers standing outside his office in central Seoul.[&#8202;](#ref-back-105-0)[\[104\]](#refnote-105)[&#8202;](#ref-back-106-0)[\[105\]](#refnote-106)

On 9 December, Police Commissioner Cho testified before the Judiciary Committee of the National Assembly that he had received a call from DCC Commander Yeo sometime between 22:30 and 22:40 on 3 December, asking him to track the locations of politicians and others to carry out their arrests. The list of targets was consistent with what NIS Deputy Director Hong had revealed the previous week, except PPP leader Han Dong-hoon was added to the list later. Commissioner Cho said that he ignored the orders, on the grounds that location tracking itself was illegal and that court-issued arrest warrants were required to carry out arrests.[&#8202;](#ref-back-107-0)[\[106\]](#refnote-107) On 13 December, a lawyer representing Commissioner Cho said that Yoon had also ordered the arrest of Seoul Central District Court Judge Kim Dong-hyun, who had acquitted Lee Jae Myung of perjury charges in November 2024.[&#8202;](#ref-back-108-0)[\[107\]](#refnote-108)

On 14 February 2025, the _Hankyoreh_ revealed that former Defense Intelligence Command leader Roh Sang-won's personal notebook contained plans to arrest "500-or-so people", which were not carried out on the night of 3 December. Other figures and alleged "leftist" judges and celebrities were included, while the notebook highlighted how the individuals would be "collected" and sent to detention centers. In addition to the names above as instructed by DCC commander Yeo, the individuals that were listed in the notebook included former President [Moon Jae-in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_Jae-in "Moon Jae-in"), Reform Party leader [Lee Jun-seok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Jun-seok "Lee Jun-seok"), [Seo Young-kyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seo_Young-kyo "Seo Young-kyo"), [Ko Min-jung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ko_Min-jung "Ko Min-jung"), [Youn Kun-young](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youn_Kun-young "Youn Kun-young"), former Justice Ministers and current DPK legislators [Choo Mi-ae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choo_Mi-ae "Choo Mi-ae") and [Park Beom-kye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Park_Beom-kye "Park Beom-kye"), former [Chiefs of Staff to the President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_Staff_to_the_President_(South_Korea) "Chief of Staff to the President (South Korea)") [Noh Young-min](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noh_Young-min "Noh Young-min") and [Im Jong-seok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Im_Jong-seok "Im Jong-seok"), Judge Yoo Chang-hun, comedian [Kim Je-dong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Je-dong "Kim Je-dong"), former football star [Cha Bum-kun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cha_Bum-kun "Cha Bum-kun"), political commentator [Rhyu Si-min](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhyu_Si-min "Rhyu Si-min"), former Prime Minister [Lee Hae-chan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Hae-chan "Lee Hae-chan"), and former [Marine Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Korea_Marine_Corps "Republic of Korea Marine Corps") investigator Park Jung-hun.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Hankyoreh_-_Noh_Sang-won_notebook-1)[\[43\]](#refnote-Hankyoreh_-_Noh_Sang-won_notebook)[&#8202;](#ref-back-YD-2)[\[5\]](#refnote-YD)

### Orders for media blackout and cutting water and power to media outlets

[National Fire Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Fire_Agency_(South_Korea) "National Fire Agency (South Korea)") Commissioner Heo Seok-Gon testified before the National Assembly on 13 January 2025 that he received a phone call from Interior Minister Lee Sang-min to cooperate with the National Police Agency to cut off power and water to the offices of liberal-leaning media outlets and critics of Yoon's government, _[The Hankyoreh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hankyoreh "The Hankyoreh")_ and the _[Kyunghyang Shinmun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyunghyang_Shinmun "Kyunghyang Shinmun")_ papers, and [MBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munhwa_Broadcasting_Corporation "Munhwa Broadcasting Corporation") TV station.[&#8202;](#ref-back-109-0)[\[108\]](#refnote-109)[&#8202;](#ref-back-110-0)[\[109\]](#refnote-110) Heo confirmed that, ultimately, no action was taken.[&#8202;](#ref-back-111-0)[\[110\]](#refnote-111)

When prosecutors indicted Yoon for insurrection in February 2025, charges revealed that in addition to the three targets, Yoon ordered power and water to be cut for broadcaster [JTBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JTBC "JTBC") and the pollster Flower Research. The indictment detailed Yoon directing the interior minister Lee at 22:30 on 3 December to halt power and water services to five organizations in order to instigate a "media blackout". Lee called National Police Agency Commissioner General Cho at 23:34 on the evening martial law was declared to check on the actions taken by the police, before calling National Fire Agency Commissioner Heo at 23:37 to inform him that the police would be deployed to the organizations around midnight and to cooperate with the National Police Agency to shut off power and water. Heo passed along the orders to Deputy Commissioner Lee Yeong-pal, who then called Hwang Gi-seok, the head of the Seoul Metropolitan Fire and Disaster Headquarters, and asked him several times to cooperate with any requests from the National Police Agency in connection with the martial law proclamation. Heo himself reportedly called Hwang at 23:50 to check whether the National Police Agency had asked for help.[&#8202;](#ref-back-112-0)[\[111\]](#refnote-112)

### Decree

At 23:25, on 3 December, Martial Law Commander Army General Park An-su issued the following decree which retroactively took effect at 23:00:[&#8202;](#ref-back-113-0)[\[112\]](#refnote-113)[&#8202;](#ref-back-114-0)[\[113\]](#refnote-114)[&#8202;](#ref-back-115-0)[\[114\]](#refnote-115)

> Martial Law Command proclaims the following as of 23:00 on 3 December 2024, in order to protect liberal democracy from anti-state forces active within the free Republic of Korea and their threats to subvert the state, and to ensure public safety.
>
> 1. All political activities, including activities of the National Assembly, local councils, political parties, and political association, rallies and demonstrations, are prohibited.
> 2. All acts that deny or attempt to overthrow the free democratic system are prohibited. The dissemination of fake news, manipulation of public opinion, and false propaganda are prohibited.
> 3. All media and publications are subject to the control of Martial Law Command.
> 4. Strikes, work stoppages, and rallies that incite social chaos are prohibited.
> 5. Trainee doctors and all other medical personnel, who are on strike or have left their worksites \[ _see [2024 South Korean medical crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_South_Korean_medical_crisis "2024 South Korean medical crisis")_\], must return to their positions within 48 hours and work faithfully. Those who violate the order will face punishment in accordance with the Martial Law Act.
> 6. Innocent ordinary citizens, excluding anti-state forces and other subversive forces, will be subject to measures to minimize inconvenience in their daily lives.
>
> Violators of the proclamation may be arrested, detained and searched without a warrant in accordance with Article 9 of the Martial Law Act (Special Measures Authority of the Martial Law Commander), and will be punished in accordance with Article 14 of the Martial Law Act (Penalties).

—Martial Law Commander, Army General Park An-su

Yoon's martial law decree went further than those of previous military dictators by suspending the National Assembly. However, it stopped short of ordering curfews or the closure of universities.[&#8202;](#ref-back-echoes-0)[\[115\]](#refnote-echoes) The unsealed indictment of Defense Minister Kim on 27 December revealed that he had begun drafting the martial law decree on 24 November, by using reference to the martial law documents drafted under the leadership of the Defense Security Command during the Park Geun-hye impeachment in March 2017 and past martial law proclamations. President Yoon ordered a nighttime curfew reference to be dropped from the decree.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kim_Yong-hyun_indictment-2)[\[21\]](#refnote-Kim_Yong-hyun_indictment)

### Immediate impact

The announcement of martial law was met by surprise and panic amongst the South Korean public.[&#8202;](#ref-back-117-0)[\[116\]](#refnote-117) Following the declaration, the government said that activities in educational institutions and transport services would continue to operate normally.[&#8202;](#ref-back-118-0)[\[117\]](#refnote-118)[&#8202;](#ref-back-119-0)[\[118\]](#refnote-119) [Panic-buying](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panic-buying "Panic-buying") occurred in convenience stores nationwide.[&#8202;](#ref-back-120-0)[\[119\]](#refnote-120) Demand for [Telegram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telegram_(software) "Telegram (software)") and [VPNs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VPN "VPN") increased after disruptions to internet portal [Naver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naver "Naver") prompted fears of government censorship, although Naver and [Kakao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kakao "Kakao") attributed this to an increase in user traffic. The [Ministry of Science and ICT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Science_and_ICT "Ministry of Science and ICT") said it had not received any requests related to martial law.[&#8202;](#ref-back-121-0)[\[120\]](#refnote-121)

Following the announcement, the [Korean won](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Korean_won "South Korean won") fell to 1,444 against the US dollar, its weakest level in 25 months. It later rose to around 1,420, still weaker than the previous day's rate of 1,403.[&#8202;](#ref-back-122-0)[\[121\]](#refnote-122)[&#8202;](#ref-back-123-0)[\[122\]](#refnote-123) The [iShares MSCI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IShares "IShares") South Korea [ETF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exchange-traded_fund "Exchange-traded fund") decreased by 5%. Franklin [FTSE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FTSE_Group "FTSE Group") South Korea ETF declined by 4.4% and Matthews Korea Active ETF fell 4.5%.[&#8202;](#ref-back-124-0)[\[123\]](#refnote-124) A [central bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_Korea "Bank of Korea") official said it was preparing measures to stabilize the market if needed. Finance Minister and Deputy Prime Minister [Choi Sang-mok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choi_Sang-mok "Choi Sang-mok") convened an emergency meeting among top economic officials.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kim-Sam-1)[\[18\]](#refnote-Kim-Sam)

### Immediate reaction of politicians

[Lee Jae Myung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Jae_Myung "Lee Jae Myung") climbing the National Assembly building fence at around 23:00 KST[&#8202;](#ref-back-No-0)[\[124\]](#refnote-No)

National Assembly Speaker [Woo Won-shik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woo_Won-shik "Woo Won-shik") announced he would convene a plenary session immediately to revoke the martial law order and called for all lawmakers to gather at the National Assembly.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kim-Tong-Hyung-1)[\[13\]](#refnote-Kim-Tong-Hyung) All main parties, including Yoon's People Power Party, opposed the martial law declaration. PPP leader [Han Dong-hoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_Dong-hoon "Han Dong-hoon") said: "The president's martial law declaration is wrong. We will stop it along with the people".[&#8202;](#ref-back-126-0)[\[125\]](#refnote-126) Seoul mayor [Oh Se-hoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oh_Se-hoon "Oh Se-hoon"), a member of the PPP, also opposed the declaration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-127-0)[\[126\]](#refnote-127) PPP floor leader Choo Kyung-ho said he only found out about the decree from the news.[&#8202;](#ref-back-128-0)[\[127\]](#refnote-128) Controversially, Choo made an announcement to PPP legislators to report at party headquarters for a meeting instead of the National Assembly to vote down the martial law contrary to Han Dong-hoon and his faction,[&#8202;](#ref-back-129-0)[\[128\]](#refnote-129) which led to allegations that he was colluding with Yoon's martial law plot. The special counsel bill passed by the National Assembly a week later to investigate key figures of the martial law declaration included Choo as a subject for investigation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-130-0)[\[129\]](#refnote-130)

Lee Jae Myung, the leader of the DPK, urged citizens to gather at the [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_Proceeding_Hall "National Assembly Proceeding Hall") and declared that Yoon "is no longer the president of South Korea". Lee began livestreaming on his [YouTube](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouTube "YouTube") channel around 22:50 as he traveled to the Assembly, telling 70,000 live viewers "There's no justification for declaring martial law. We cannot let the military rule the country".[&#8202;](#ref-back-No-1)[\[124\]](#refnote-No) The [Incheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incheon "Incheon") branch of the DPK criticized the declaration as the beginning of an "era of Yoon dictatorship". [Rebuilding Korea Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebuilding_Korea_Party "Rebuilding Korea Party") leader [Cho Kuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cho_Kuk "Cho Kuk") called the declaration of martial law "illegal" and said it met conditions for the impeachment of Yoon and Defense Minister Kim.[&#8202;](#ref-back-131-0)[\[130\]](#refnote-131) [Lee Jun-seok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Jun-seok "Lee Jun-seok"), leader of the [Reform Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_Party_(South_Korea) "Reform Party (South Korea)"), called for Yoon to be expelled from the PPP, while Gyeonggi Province governor [Kim Dong-yeon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Dong-yeon_(politician) "Kim Dong-yeon (politician)") called for Yoon's arrest.[&#8202;](#ref-back-132-0)[\[131\]](#refnote-132) The [Korean Confederation of Trade Unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_Confederation_of_Trade_Unions "Korean Confederation of Trade Unions"), the largest trade union group in the country, called for a [general strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_strike "General strike") to reverse the declaration of martial law and impeach the president.[&#8202;](#ref-back-133-0)[\[132\]](#refnote-133)

Former President [Moon Jae-in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_Jae-in "Moon Jae-in") addressed the military as a former [commander-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander-in-chief "Commander-in-chief") in a [Facebook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Facebook "Facebook") post, urging them to respect the will of the people, not to act against the National Assembly, and to adhere strictly to constitutional principles. He called on the military to focus on its legitimate duties, safeguarding national security without compromising democratic institutions.[&#8202;](#ref-back-134-0)[\[133\]](#refnote-134)[&#8202;](#ref-back-135-0)[\[134\]](#refnote-135) A handful of conservative figures supported the decree, such as former prime minister [Hwang Kyo-ahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hwang_Kyo-ahn "Hwang Kyo-ahn"), who called for the arrest of both Woo Won-shik and Han Dong-hoon. The mayor of [Daegu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daegu "Daegu"), [Hong Joon-pyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Joon-pyo "Hong Joon-pyo"), did not explicitly take sides, but said he understood "Yoon's loyalty" while describing his actions as reckless and "making a scene".[&#8202;](#ref-back-136-0)[\[135\]](#refnote-136)

### National Assembly vote

[![See caption](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/da/2024_South_Korean_martial_law_voiceofseoullive07.png/500px-2024_South_Korean_martial_law_voiceofseoullive07.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:2024_South_Korean_martial_law_voiceofseoullive07.png)

National Assembly staff and aides barricade the main assembly entrance with furniture against approaching troops

[![See caption](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/%EC%84%9C%EC%9A%B8_%EA%B3%84%EC%97%84%EB%A0%B9.png/500px-%EC%84%9C%EC%9A%B8_%EA%B3%84%EC%97%84%EB%A0%B9.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:%EC%84%9C%EC%9A%B8_%EA%B3%84%EC%97%84%EB%A0%B9.png)

Civilians blocking military vehicles from entering Yeouido

Speaker Woo Won-shik announcing the National Assembly voted 190–0 to end martial law

[![See caption](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/2024_South_Korea_National_Assembly_vote_on_motion_to_lift_martial_law.svg/500px-2024_South_Korea_National_Assembly_vote_on_motion_to_lift_martial_law.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:2024_South_Korea_National_Assembly_vote_on_motion_to_lift_martial_law.svg)

National Assembly vote summary of lifting martial law

Yes

Not present

[![Wikisource logo](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Wikisource-logo.svg/120px-Wikisource-logo.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Wikisource-logo.svg)

[Wikisource](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikisource "Wikisource") has original text related to this article:

_**[Resolution to Demand Lifting of Martial Law](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Translation:Resolution%20to%20Demand%20Lifting%20of%20Extraordinary%20Martial%20Law%20(2024) "wikisource:Translation:Resolution to Demand Lifting of Extraordinary Martial Law (2024)")**_

_**[National Assembly Minutes on 4 Dec](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Translation:National%20Assembly%20Plenary%20Session%20Minutes%20(South%20Korea)/22nd/418th%20Session/15th%20Meeting "wikisource:Translation:National Assembly Plenary Session Minutes (South Korea)/22nd/418th Session/15th Meeting")**_

From 22:48, martial law forces initially requested permission from the Capital Defense Command several times to allow helicopters to fly over restricted airspace to seize the National Assembly. Approval was repeatedly rejected on the grounds that the purpose of the flights was unknown. Permission was only approved at 23:31. Twenty-four helicopter flights carried 240 troops to the National Assembly until 01:18 the next morning.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Joongang_timeline_from_testimonies-4)[\[49\]](#refnote-Joongang_timeline_from_testimonies)

The [Seoul Metropolitan Police Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seoul_Metropolitan_Police_Agency "Seoul Metropolitan Police Agency") blocked all entrances to the National Assembly by 23:04 and prevented lawmakers from reaching the plenary session to overturn the martial law declaration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-KoreaPro-1)[\[51\]](#refnote-KoreaPro) Lawmakers instead maneuvered around police barricades to enter the Assembly, with some assistance from civilians. At around 23:00, Lee Jae Myung livestreamed himself climbing over a fence to gain access,[&#8202;](#ref-back-No-2)[\[124\]](#refnote-No)[&#8202;](#ref-back-137-0)[\[136\]](#refnote-137)[&#8202;](#ref-back-138-0)[\[137\]](#refnote-138)[&#8202;](#ref-back-139-0)[\[138\]](#refnote-139) and Speaker Woo also climbed a fence to enter the Assembly premises.[&#8202;](#ref-back-140-0)[\[139\]](#refnote-140) Lee Jun-seok, leader of the Reform Party, was seen confronting police officers for obstructing lawmakers,[&#8202;](#ref-back-141-0)[\[140\]](#refnote-141) but recounted later that some police officers appeared not to know what to do.[&#8202;](#ref-back-142-0)[\[141\]](#refnote-142)

At 23:50, [UH-60P Black Hawk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sikorsky_UH-60_Black_Hawk#Variants "Sikorsky UH-60 Black Hawk")[&#8202;](#ref-back-143-0)[\[142\]](#refnote-143) military helicopters approached the grounds, prompting lawmakers and aides to build furniture barricades inside.[&#8202;](#ref-back-KoreaPro-2)[\[51\]](#refnote-KoreaPro) Around 23:57, the 707th SMG appeared in front of the [National Assembly Proceeding Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_Proceeding_Hall "National Assembly Proceeding Hall"), attempting to enter the building, leading to scuffles with aides and legislative staff who tried to prevent their entry. DPK spokeswoman [Ahn Gwi-ryeong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahn_Gwi-ryeong "Ahn Gwi-ryeong") tried to seize a rifle from a soldier before the soldier pointed it at her briefly, prompting her to berate the soldier, saying "Aren't you ashamed?", as the soldier walked away.[&#8202;](#ref-back-144-0)[\[143\]](#refnote-144) The video of the incident was captured on a [livestream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Live_streaming "Live streaming") by [OhmyNews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OhmyNews "OhmyNews") and viewed millions of times on [Twitter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter "Twitter").[&#8202;](#ref-back-145-0)[\[144\]](#refnote-145) She later told [BBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC "BBC") Korean Service that "I didn't think... I just knew we had to stop this".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Ku-0)[\[145\]](#refnote-Ku)

At 00:45,[&#8202;](#ref-back-KoreaPro-3)[\[51\]](#refnote-KoreaPro) around 300[&#8202;](#ref-back-Ye-1)[\[62\]](#refnote-Ye) military personnel entered the National Assembly building, broke windows and attempted to enter the main hall, where Speaker Woo was about to begin the plenary session to revoke the martial law decree. In response, staff sprayed them with fire extinguishers and successfully stopped their entry. Some soldiers attempted to enter through the fourth floor, but were stopped by employees.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Lee-0)[\[146\]](#refnote-Lee) Soldiers broke the windows of several MPs' offices, while injuries occurred during confrontations inside the building.[&#8202;](#ref-back-148-0)[\[147\]](#refnote-148) Ultimately, the soldiers were unable to access the main session hall, whose entrances were barricaded using furniture installed by staff.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Ku-1)[\[145\]](#refnote-Ku)[&#8202;](#ref-back-149-0)[\[148\]](#refnote-149) At least three helicopters also landed on the Assembly grounds while two others were seen hovering above.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kim-Tong-Hyung-2)[\[13\]](#refnote-Kim-Tong-Hyung) [Armored military vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_vehicle "Military vehicle") were seen on the streets,[&#8202;](#ref-back-150-0)[\[149\]](#refnote-150) while the martial law command also ordered the eviction of the Presidential Office press corps from its building in Seoul.[&#8202;](#ref-back-151-0)[\[150\]](#refnote-151)

The National Assembly commenced the emergency session at 00:48 on 4 December.[&#8202;](#ref-back-KTimes-emergency-0)[\[151\]](#refnote-KTimes-emergency) At 01:02,[&#8202;](#ref-back-153-0)[\[152\]](#refnote-153)[&#8202;](#ref-back-154-0)[\[153\]](#refnote-154) with 190 of 300 lawmakers present, they voted unanimously to lift martial law.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Sky_News-1)[\[58\]](#refnote-Sky_News) Those who voted included 172 opposition MPs and 18 members of a PPP[&#8202;](#ref-back-asseminv-1)[\[4\]](#refnote-asseminv) faction supporting Han Dong-hoon.[&#8202;](#ref-back-han_unimpeached_herald-0)[\[154\]](#refnote-han_unimpeached_herald) Following the vote, Speaker Woo Won-shik called for the military and police to leave the Assembly as "declaration of extraordinary martial law is now void" while reiterating that the president did not notify the National Assembly on implementing martial law as prescribed in the Constitution. Lee Jae Myung said that the DPK would remain in the Assembly until the president lifted martial law. Lee called for police and soldiers to return to their positions and not abide with Yoon's "illegal act".[&#8202;](#ref-back-156-0)[\[155\]](#refnote-156)[&#8202;](#ref-back-157-0)[\[156\]](#refnote-157) They were also joined by members of the PPP.[&#8202;](#ref-back-KTimes-emergency-1)[\[151\]](#refnote-KTimes-emergency) The plenary session was only adjourned at 05:54 upon confirmation from the State Council that martial law had been withdrawn.

_The Korea Times_ and opposition parties identified units of the security forces involved in the incursion at the Assembly as the [1st Airborne Special Forces Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1st_Airborne_Special_Forces_Brigade "1st Airborne Special Forces Brigade") of the Army Special Warfare Command and the Seoul Metropolitan Police Agency, with the National Police Agency believed to have been bypassed by the declaration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-KTimes-overlook-1)[\[59\]](#refnote-KTimes-overlook) The National Police Agency declared a Level B Emergency.[&#8202;](#ref-back-158-0)[\[157\]](#refnote-158) The _[Kyunghyang Shinmun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyunghyang_Shinmun "Kyunghyang Shinmun")_ published images of soldiers next to boxes of what appeared to be live bullets and ammunition at the Assembly.[&#8202;](#ref-back-159-0)[\[158\]](#refnote-159) Protests against martial law were organized in [Gwangju](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwangju "Gwangju"), where memories of the 1980 [Gwangju Uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwangju_Uprising "Gwangju Uprising") and subsequent crackdown remain fresh.[&#8202;](#ref-back-160-0)[\[159\]](#refnote-160)

On 5 December, an anonymous special forces soldier told the media that the soldiers sent to the National Assembly only learned of the martial law declaration from the news.[&#8202;](#ref-back-161-0)[\[160\]](#refnote-161) Other soldiers have said they were kept in the dark to varying degrees; most were not even told their destination until _en route_, and were only given a specific mission after arrival.
