One said he felt betrayed by his superiors. Many were reluctant and deliberately slow in carrying out orders.[&#8202;](#ref-back-162-0)[\[161\]](#refnote-162)

On 6 December, Lieutenant General Kwak Jong-geun, Chief of the Army Special Warfare Command, in an interview by DPK legislators [Kim Byung-joo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Byung-joo_(general) "Kim Byung-joo (general)") and Park Sun-won, revealed that the defense minister gave orders for troops to drag out the legislators, corroborating the earlier reports. Kwak said he prohibited giving live ammunition to individual soldiers as he witnessed "unjustified scenes" during the deployment, and added that "based on my judgment, dragging lawmakers out was clearly an illegal act"; he defied the orders, ordering the troops not to enter the plenary hall.[&#8202;](#ref-back-163-0)[\[162\]](#refnote-163) Brigadier General Lee Sang-hyun, commander of the [1st Special Forces Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1st_Special_Forces_Brigade_(South_Korea) "1st Special Forces Brigade (South Korea)") confirmed that they were deployed to the National Assembly, with deployment of two battalions, consisting of about 250 soldiers in total. He confirmed that orders were given to remove the lawmakers using means such as breaking down doors or cutting electricity, while confirming that Kwak gave orders not to give live ammunition.[&#8202;](#ref-back-164-0)[\[163\]](#refnote-164) On a subsequent inquiry by the National Assembly on 10 December with a huge delegation of military officers summoned for questioning by legislators, Kwak said that the president personally called him to demand "break\[&#8202;](#ref-back-165-0)[ing\] open the door, and drag the lawmakers out" and further added that he was made aware of plans for martial law on 1 December, two days before the announcement. Kwak said that the prosecutors in charge of investigating Yoon's martial law declaration were framing their questions in a way that held former Defense Minister Kim accountable, and shifting the blame away from Yoon.[\[164\]](#refnote-165)

Subsequent investigations revealed that Yoon ordered Commander Kwak Jong-geun and Capital Defense Commander Lee Jin-woo to break through the plenary chamber doors to drag the lawmakers out at 00:40 to 00:50 hours, which was too late as the lawmakers had already begun the session to end martial law.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Joongang_timeline_from_testimonies-5)[\[49\]](#refnote-Joongang_timeline_from_testimonies) Yoon also called Police Commissioner General Cho Ji-ho several times and instructed him to arrest all the lawmakers trying to enter the National Assembly, saying "Bring them in. It's illegal. All the lawmakers are violating the proclamation. Arrest them." In the indictment of Kim Yong-hyun on 27 December by the special prosecution team, it was also revealed that Yoon told Commander Lee: "Break down the doors, even if it means shooting." When he was notified the lawmakers had begun the session to end martial law, Yoon ordered Special Warfare Commander Kwak to "break down the door with an axe and go in and get them all out". Defense Minister Kim also ordered: "Stop the number of National Assembly members so that it is less than 150. Bring the National Assembly members out." He also prioritized the arrests and detention of Lee Jae Myung, [Woo Won-shik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woo_Won-shik "Woo Won-shik"), and Han Dong-hoon from the National Assembly. Upon finding out about the National Assembly vote being passed, Yoon denied it was legitimate, and told Commander Lee, "It's not even confirmed that 190 people came in. Even if martial law was lifted, I just have to declare martial law two or three more times, so keep going."[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kim_Yong-hyun_indictment-3)[\[21\]](#refnote-Kim_Yong-hyun_indictment)

The group messenger chatroom of the counter-intelligence arrest team, made public by the prosecution, contained the following message: "Cancel all existing detention quotas. All teams should first arrest Woo Won-sik, Lee Jae Myung, and Han Dong-hoon, whichever team you see, and then move them to the Subangsa detention facility." There was also a message saying, "Secure new recruits through the operational unit on site, then take them over and detain them at the Subangsa. Use ropes and handcuffs."[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kim_Yong-hyun_indictment-4)[\[21\]](#refnote-Kim_Yong-hyun_indictment) Confrontations took place at the complex's main gate between security forces and civilians.[&#8202;](#ref-back-166-0)[\[165\]](#refnote-166) Crowds gathered as soon as martial law was declared, chanting slogans such as "lift martial law", "take down the dictatorship", "no violence" and "impeach Yoon".[&#8202;](#ref-back-KoreaPro-4)[\[51\]](#refnote-KoreaPro)

CCTV footage released in February 2025 showed that in addition to troops smashing windows on the second floor of the National Assembly's main building on the night of martial law, a few troops were caught on camera opening a switchboard on the basement floor at 01:06 (five minutes after the National Assembly voted to rescind martial law), turning off both the main power and the backup power for a portion of the building. Half of the first basement floor of the building went dark, with the blackout lasting for 5 minutes and 48 seconds. The seven soldiers who cut off the power belonged to the 707th Special Mission Group, the same elite 16-person unit that entered the National Assembly's main chamber.[&#8202;](#ref-back-167-0)[\[166\]](#refnote-167)

## Aftermath

[![Troops of the 1st Airborne Special Forces Brigade preparing to leave the National Assembly](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/South_Korean_soldiers_waiting_to_leave_the_National_Assembly.png/500px-South_Korean_soldiers_waiting_to_leave_the_National_Assembly.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:South_Korean_soldiers_waiting_to_leave_the_National_Assembly.png) Troops preparing to leave the National Assembly after the vote to end martial law

After the vote, soldiers began withdrawing;[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kim-Tong-Hyung-3)[\[13\]](#refnote-Kim-Tong-Hyung) the Speaker's office later said that they had left altogether by 01:18.[&#8202;](#ref-back-168-0)[\[167\]](#refnote-168)[&#8202;](#ref-back-169-0)[\[168\]](#refnote-169) Others began pushing back the crowd of protesters who gathered there,[&#8202;](#ref-back-Yoon-0)[\[169\]](#refnote-Yoon) which numbered around 2,000.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Lee-1)[\[146\]](#refnote-Lee) Several soldiers bowed in apology to the public over the incursion, while others consoled civilians who had engaged in confrontations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-171-0)[\[170\]](#refnote-171) Protesters began calling for Yoon's arrest and impeachment.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Yoon-1)[\[169\]](#refnote-Yoon)[&#8202;](#ref-back-172-0)[\[171\]](#refnote-172) Lee Jae Myung said the declaration of martial law was done without cabinet approval,[&#8202;](#ref-back-173-0)[\[172\]](#refnote-173) and that members of the security forces who continued to follow Yoon's martial law orders were committing "an illegal act".[&#8202;](#ref-back-174-0)[\[173\]](#refnote-174) The [Defense Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_National_Defense_(South_Korea) "Ministry of National Defense (South Korea)") said that martial law would remain in effect until ended by the president.[&#8202;](#ref-back-175-0)[\[174\]](#refnote-175)[&#8202;](#ref-back-176-0)[\[175\]](#refnote-176)

Yoon held a meeting inside the Joint Chiefs of Staff's command and control room with Defense Minister Kim Yong-hyun, Martial Law Commander Park An-su, 2nd Deputy Director of the National Security Office In Sung-hwan, Defense Secretary Choi Byung-ok, and Military Advisor to the Ministry of National Defense Kim Chul-jin. Yoon asked Kim Yong-hyun the amount of troops he deployed to the National Assembly. He responded: "about 500." Yoon replied: "I told you it's not enough. You should have sent 1,000. What are we going to do now?" While searching for another method, Yoon looked for a copy of South Korea's National Assembly Act. Yoon ultimately decided to lift the martial law following this meeting, despite there being no discussion about lifting martial law at that meeting.[&#8202;](#ref-back-jointmeet-0)[\[176\]](#refnote-jointmeet)

After martial law was lifted, the president's office told foreign media 38 hours after refusing all interviews with South Korean media that "emergency martial law was strictly enforced within the framework of the constitution and took place late at night to minimize damage to the national economy and citizens' daily lives".[&#8202;](#ref-back-178-0)[\[177\]](#refnote-178) DPK leader Park Chan-dae then told [CNN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CNN "CNN") that Yoon "cannot avoid the charge of [treason](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treason "Treason")" and that he "must step down immediately".[&#8202;](#ref-back-179-0)[\[178\]](#refnote-179) To prevent future disruptions, on 3 July 2025, the National Assembly voted to amend the country's guidelines on martial law by barring security forces from obstructing the work of MPs and entering the [National Assembly Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_Building_(South_Korea) "National Assembly Building (South Korea)") without approval from the [Speaker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speaker_of_the_National_Assembly_(South_Korea) "Speaker of the National Assembly (South Korea)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-180-0)[\[179\]](#refnote-180)

### Lifting of martial law

During a televised briefing at 04:27, Yoon announced that he would lift martial law as soon as a quorum could be obtained for a cabinet meeting, and that he had withdrawn military personnel from the National Assembly.[&#8202;](#ref-back-181-0)[\[180\]](#refnote-181) At approximately 04:30, the cabinet approved the motion to lift martial law.[&#8202;](#ref-back-182-0)[\[181\]](#refnote-182) The Martial Law Command was also disbanded.[&#8202;](#ref-back-183-0)[\[182\]](#refnote-183)[&#8202;](#ref-back-184-0)[\[183\]](#refnote-184) The military said it had not observed unusual activity in North Korea while martial law was in place.[&#8202;](#ref-back-185-0)[\[184\]](#refnote-185)

Following the lifting of martial law, the DPK held an emergency meeting at the Assembly, announcing that they would commence impeachment proceedings if Yoon did not step down. In a resolution, it said "Yoon's declaration of martial law is a clear violation of the Constitution", adding that it was "a serious act of rebellion and a perfect reason for impeachment".[&#8202;](#ref-back-186-0)[\[185\]](#refnote-186) Protests continued to be held in the Assembly and in [Gwanghwamun Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwanghwamun_Square "Gwanghwamun Square"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-187-0)[\[186\]](#refnote-187) as well as outside the [Presidential Residence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Residence_of_South_Korea "Presidential Residence of South Korea").[&#8202;](#ref-back-188-0)[\[187\]](#refnote-188) Candlelight rallies and related activities were held in cities across South Korea.[&#8202;](#ref-back-189-0)[\[188\]](#refnote-189) Smaller rallies were held in support of Yoon.[&#8202;](#ref-back-investigations-0)[\[189\]](#refnote-investigations)

### Protests against Yoon Suk Yeol

**Protests against Yoon Suk Yeol**

| [![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8b/Calls_for_President_Yoon_Suk_Yeol_to_resign_at_Nat%27l_Assembly_on_December_4%2C_2024_01.jpg/960px-Calls_for_President_Yoon_Suk_Yeol_to_resign_at_Nat%27l_Assembly_on_December_4%2C_2024_01.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Calls_for_President_Yoon_Suk_Yeol_to_resign_at_Nat'l_Assembly_on_December_4,_2024_01.jpg)<br>[Lee Jae Myung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Jae_Myung "Lee Jae Myung") at the emergency rally held at the National Assembly on 4December, with [Cho Kuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cho_Kuk "Cho Kuk") to his left |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Date | 3December2024(2024-12-03) – 4April2025(2025-04-04) |  |
| Location | South Korea |  |
| Caused by | Declaration of martial law by President [Yoon Suk Yeol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoon_Suk_Yeol "Yoon Suk Yeol") |  |
| Goals | Resignation or [impeachment of Yoon Suk Yeol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impeachment_of_Yoon_Suk_Yeol "Impeachment of Yoon Suk Yeol") |  |
| Status | Protests are successful, Yoon Suk Yeol is impeached by the National Assembly, and removed from office by the Constitutional Court of Korea, protests ended |  |
| Number |  |  |
|  |  |  |
| **Protesters:** 1,000,000 (maximum)[&#8202;](#ref-back-straitstimes.com-0)[\[190\]](#refnote-straitstimes.com) | **Counter-protesters:** 30,000 (maximum)[&#8202;](#ref-back-192-0)[\[191\]](#refnote-192) |  |
| 150,000 police deployed (maximum)[&#8202;](#ref-back-straitstimes.com-1)[\[190\]](#refnote-straitstimes.com) |  |  |

Inspector General of the [Ministry of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Justice_(South_Korea) "Ministry of Justice (South Korea)") Ryu Hyuk resigned in protest shortly after attending a meeting convened on martial law by Justice Minister [Park Sung-jae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Park_Sung-jae "Park Sung-jae").[&#8202;](#ref-back-193-0)[\[192\]](#refnote-193)[&#8202;](#ref-back-194-0)[\[193\]](#refnote-194) Shortly after Yoon lifted martial law, the opposition began to [consider his impeachment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_impeachment_motion_against_Yoon_Suk_Yeol "2024 impeachment motion against Yoon Suk Yeol").[&#8202;](#ref-back-195-0)[\[194\]](#refnote-195)[&#8202;](#ref-back-196-0)[\[195\]](#refnote-196) Some South Korean analysts described the episode as a self-coup attempt to seize power.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Foreign_Policy_magazine_self-coup-1)[\[48\]](#refnote-Foreign_Policy_magazine_self-coup) In the early hours of 4 December, dozens of Yoon's aides resigned _en masse_ following the martial law declaration and recantation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-197-0)[\[196\]](#refnote-197) That same day, the [Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Korea "Supreme Court of Korea") announced it would investigate whether the declaration was illegal, given that he had failed to abide by mandatory provisions such as notifying both the cabinet and the legislature beforehand.[&#8202;](#ref-back-198-0)[\[197\]](#refnote-198) On 6 December, the [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Korea "Constitutional Court of Korea") also opened an investigation into the legality of Yoon's martial law declaration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-199-0)[\[198\]](#refnote-199)

The leadership of the PPP discussed expelling Yoon from the party during a meeting.[&#8202;](#ref-back-200-0)[\[199\]](#refnote-200) Han Dong-hoon urged Yoon to immediately fire Defense Minister Kim for proposing the idea.[&#8202;](#ref-back-201-0)[\[200\]](#refnote-201) He later asked Yoon to leave the PPP through presidential officials and the prime minister.[&#8202;](#ref-back-202-0)[\[201\]](#refnote-202) The DPK confirmed they would initiate impeachment proceedings against Yoon, Kim, and Interior Minister [Lee Sang-min](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Sang-min_(lawyer) "Lee Sang-min (lawyer)") on 5 December if Yoon did not resign.[&#8202;](#ref-back-203-0)[\[202\]](#refnote-203) Kim apologized and took responsibility for the soldiers' actions. He offered his resignation,[&#8202;](#ref-back-204-0)[\[203\]](#refnote-204) which was accepted by Yoon.[&#8202;](#ref-back-205-0)[\[204\]](#refnote-205) In a separate interview, Kim said that the deployment of soldiers to the National Election Commission was aimed at assessing the "necessity of an investigation into alleged election fraud".[&#8202;](#ref-back-206-0)[\[205\]](#refnote-206) In response, the commission denounced the incursion as a violation of the Constitution and pledged to take legal action.[&#8202;](#ref-back-207-0)[\[206\]](#refnote-207) At noon on 4 December, Prime Minister Han Duck-soo held a meeting with party leaders and Yoon's remaining cabinet members to discuss the fallout of the martial law declaration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-208-0)[\[207\]](#refnote-208) He subsequently apologized and took full responsibility for what had happened.[&#8202;](#ref-back-209-0)[\[208\]](#refnote-209)

Later on 4 December, all of the major newspapers in South Korea and the National Union of Media Workers unanimously condemned Yoon and called for his arrest, saying the martial law was illegal and an attempted repeat of the brutal coups of the 1980s.[&#8202;](#ref-back-210-0)[\[209\]](#refnote-210) At the same time, multiple South Korean celebrities also heavily condemned Yoon.[&#8202;](#ref-back-211-0)[\[210\]](#refnote-211) The [Catholic Bishops' Conference of Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Bishops'_Conference_of_Korea "Catholic Bishops' Conference of Korea"), the [Korean Methodist Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_Methodist_Church "Korean Methodist Church"), the [National Council of Churches in Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Council_of_Churches_in_Korea "National Council of Churches in Korea"), and the Korean Church Human Rights Center criticized the declaration of martial law,[&#8202;](#ref-back-212-0)[\[211\]](#refnote-212) while the national association of [Won Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Won_Buddhism "Won Buddhism") clerics called for Yoon's impeachment.[&#8202;](#ref-back-213-0)[\[212\]](#refnote-213)

A joint declaration was signed by 370 professors and researchers at [Korea University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korea_University "Korea University") calling for Yoon's impeachment.[&#8202;](#ref-back-214-0)[\[213\]](#refnote-214) The emergency medical professors of [Seoul National University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seoul_National_University "Seoul National University") and [Seoul National University Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seoul_National_University_Hospital "Seoul National University Hospital") also criticized martial law and its attempt to halt the [ongoing doctors' strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_South_Korean_medical_crisis "2024 South Korean medical crisis") and supported Yoon's impeachment.[&#8202;](#ref-back-215-0)[\[214\]](#refnote-215) In particular, the term "cheodan" (처단) in Article 5 of the decree on martial law has sparked strong reactions from both medical professionals and the general public in Korea.[&#8202;](#ref-back-216-0)[\[215\]](#refnote-216)[&#8202;](#ref-back-217-0)[\[216\]](#refnote-217) Although it was translated as "punishment", its real-world usage aligns more closely with "execution", fueling significant controversy.[&#8202;](#ref-back-218-0)[\[217\]](#refnote-218) The chair of the Chungam High School Foundation called Yoon and Kim Yong-hyun "shameful graduates".[&#8202;](#ref-back-koreatimes.co.kr-0)[\[218\]](#refnote-koreatimes.co.kr) The school suspended its uniform policy for students amid concerns over harassment, adding that some of its school bus drivers had been harassed by passersby and that it had received hundreds of protest calls.[&#8202;](#ref-back-220-0)[\[219\]](#refnote-220) Its student council issued a statement supporting Yoon's impeachment.[&#8202;](#ref-back-221-0)[\[220\]](#refnote-221) Five MPs of the PPP from Han Dong-hoon's faction expressed support for amending the constitution to move Yoon's end of term from 2027 to 2026.[&#8202;](#ref-back-222-0)[\[221\]](#refnote-222) Some PPP legislators called for amendments shifting power from the president to the prime minister and allowing for power-sharing between parties under a coalition government.[&#8202;](#ref-back-223-0)[\[222\]](#refnote-223)

On 5 December, Kim Min-seok, who had previously warned of a possible declaration of martial law in August, said that he was "100 percent certain" that Yoon would try and declare martial law again, attributing Yoon's motivations as to protect himself and his wife from ongoing investigations. He also attributed the failure of 3 December declaration to popular resistance and the incompetence of Defense Minister Kim Yong-hyun.[&#8202;](#ref-back-224-0)[\[223\]](#refnote-224) The Center for Military Human Rights Korea also warned of such a possibility, citing the Army restricting leave for some officers and implementing stringent regulations effective until 8 December, which coincided with the period that proposals to impeach Yoon are being discussed in the National Assembly.[&#8202;](#ref-back-225-0)[\[224\]](#refnote-225) On 6 December, the Defense Ministry and the Joint Chiefs of Staff denied the allegations and said that they would not comply with a second declaration of martial law,[&#8202;](#ref-back-226-0)[\[225\]](#refnote-226) while the DPK said all 170 of its lawmakers will remain on emergency standby within the National Assembly premises to vote down future declarations of martial law.[&#8202;](#ref-back-227-0)[\[226\]](#refnote-227) The National Assembly Secretariat barred officials from the Defense Ministry, police and the NIS from entering its buildings "for the time being".[&#8202;](#ref-back-228-0)[\[227\]](#refnote-228)

On the afternoon of 6 December, rumors that Yoon would visit the Assembly for a meeting with the PPP prompted opposition lawmakers and staff to gather at the building's main rotunda to block his entry. Additional entry restrictions were imposed on other visitors, and the complex's day care center was advised to send children home early. The Presidential Office later said that Yoon had no plans to visit the legislature.[&#8202;](#ref-back-229-0)[\[228\]](#refnote-229) On 3 January 2025, the city government of [Gwangju](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwangju "Gwangju") raised the [state flag of Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_and_seal_of_Virginia "Flag and seal of Virginia"), which contains the inscription _[Sic semper tyrannis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sic_semper_tyrannis "Sic semper tyrannis")_, in front of the city hall, with mayor Kang Ki-jung denouncing Yoon for abuse of power. The mayor claimed to have received the flag from [the state's governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glenn_Youngkin "Glenn Youngkin") alongside a letter of appreciation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-230-0)[\[229\]](#refnote-230)

### Impeachment, arrest, and indictment

Main articles: [Impeachment of Yoon Suk Yeol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impeachment_of_Yoon_Suk_Yeol "Impeachment of Yoon Suk Yeol") and [Arrest of Yoon Suk Yeol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrest_of_Yoon_Suk_Yeol "Arrest of Yoon Suk Yeol")

[![Wikisource logo](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Wikisource-logo.svg/120px-Wikisource-logo.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Wikisource-logo.svg)

Korean [Wikisource](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikisource "Wikisource") has original text related to this article:

**[대통령(윤석열) 탄핵소추안](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/ko:%EB%8C%80%ED%86%B5%EB%A0%B9(%EC%9C%A4%EC%84%9D%EC%97%B4)%20%ED%83%84%ED%95%B5%EC%86%8C%EC%B6%94%EC%95%88 "s:ko:대통령(윤석열) 탄핵소추안")**

In the [Judiciary of South Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judiciary_of_South_Korea "Judiciary of South Korea"), matters of constitutional importance, such as impeachment trials, fall under the jurisdiction of the [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Korea "Constitutional Court of Korea"). The [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_(South_Korea) "National Assembly (South Korea)") takes role of plaintiff in an impeachment trial. Other ordinary matters, such as criminal trial, fall under the jurisdiction of ordinary courts, represented by the [Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Korea "Supreme Court of Korea"). In criminal processes, the [Supreme Prosecutors' Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Prosecutors'_Office_of_the_Republic_of_Korea "Supreme Prosecutors' Office of the Republic of Korea") takes the role of prosecutor, while the [National Police Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Police_Agency_(South_Korea) "National Police Agency (South Korea)") and the [Corruption Investigation Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_Investigation_Office_for_High-ranking_Officials "Corruption Investigation Office for High-ranking Officials") investigate the crime.

#### Impeachment by National Assembly

On 4 December, 190[&#8202;](#ref-back-233-0)[\[b\]](#refnote-233) legislators across six opposition parties submitted a motion for impeachment, intending to discuss the bill the following day;[&#8202;](#ref-back-Davies-0)[\[231\]](#refnote-Davies) the DPK later planned a vote on 7 December.[&#8202;](#ref-back-234-0)[\[232\]](#refnote-234) At a meeting with Prime Minister Han Duck-soo and PPP party leader Han Dong-hoon, Yoon said that he would not resign, there was "no wrongdoing" in his declaration, it was a "warning" to the opposition, and it was to prevent the DPK's "reckless impeachment actions". Yoon also defended plans to arrest Han Dong-hoon for going to the National Assembly.[&#8202;](#ref-back-235-0)[\[233\]](#refnote-235)[&#8202;](#ref-back-236-0)[\[234\]](#refnote-236) On 5 December, the PPP announced they would oppose impeachment, following an emergency meeting the previous evening.[&#8202;](#ref-back-237-0)[\[235\]](#refnote-237) However, at an emergency meeting on 6 December, Han Dong-hoon said it was necessary to "promptly suspend \[&#8202;](#ref-back-susyoon-0)[Yoon\] from his duties to protect the Republic of Korea", citing Yoon's orders to arrest and detain key politicians during martial law, including Han himself.[\[230\]](#refnote-susyoon)[&#8202;](#ref-back-bbc6dec-1)[\[100\]](#refnote-bbc6dec) That same day, [Cho Kyoung-tae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cho_Kyoung-tae "Cho Kyoung-tae") voiced his support for Yoon's impeachment, becoming the first MP from the PPP to do so.[&#8202;](#ref-back-bbc6dec-2)[\[100\]](#refnote-bbc6dec)

On 14 December, the National Assembly voted to impeach Yoon, with 204 lawmakers, including 12 from the PPP, supporting impeachment.[&#8202;](#ref-back-238-0)[\[236\]](#refnote-238) Yoon's presidential powers were suspended immediately upon the delivery of the impeachment resolution to the Presidential Office. Prime Minister Han Duck-soo stepped in as acting president,[&#8202;](#ref-back-239-0)[\[237\]](#refnote-239) and the impeachment motion proceeded to the Constitutional Court.[&#8202;](#ref-back-240-0)[\[238\]](#refnote-240)[&#8202;](#ref-back-241-0)[\[239\]](#refnote-241) On 27 December, 192 MPs in the National Assembly voted to [impeach Han Duck-soo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impeachment_of_Han_Duck-soo "Impeachment of Han Duck-soo") for blocking investigations against Yoon and his wife, colluding with Yoon on martial law and blocking the appointment of justices to fill vacancies in the Constitutional Court.[&#8202;](#ref-back-herald_actingimpeached-0)[\[240\]](#refnote-herald_actingimpeached)[&#8202;](#ref-back-243-0)[\[241\]](#refnote-243)[&#8202;](#ref-back-244-0)[\[242\]](#refnote-244) Despite being boycotted by the PPP, Han's impeachment was made possible with a simple majority because Han was Prime Minister rather than the elected president.[&#8202;](#ref-back-herald_actingimpeached-1)[\[240\]](#refnote-herald_actingimpeached)[&#8202;](#ref-back-cnn_actingimpeached-0)[\[243\]](#refnote-cnn_actingimpeached) This made Deputy Prime Minister and Finance Minister [Choi Sang-mok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choi_Sang-mok "Choi Sang-mok") the new acting president.[&#8202;](#ref-back-herald_actingimpeached-2)[\[240\]](#refnote-herald_actingimpeached)[&#8202;](#ref-back-cnn_actingimpeached-1)[\[243\]](#refnote-cnn_actingimpeached) On 21 March 2025, the DPK and four other opposition parties submitted a motion in the National Assembly to impeach Choi, citing charges that included abetting Yoon's martial law declaration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-246-0)[\[244\]](#refnote-246) On 24 March 2025, the Constitutional Court overturned Han's impeachment, reinstating him as acting president.[&#8202;](#ref-back-han_unimpeached_herald-1)[\[154\]](#refnote-han_unimpeached_herald) On 1 May, Choi resigned minutes before the National Assembly was set to vote on his impeachment, prompting the suspension of the proceedings.[&#8202;](#ref-back-247-0)[\[245\]](#refnote-247)

#### Initial legal investigations

See also: [Arrest of Yoon Suk Yeol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrest_of_Yoon_Suk_Yeol "Arrest of Yoon Suk Yeol")

The DPK sought Defense Minister Kim Yong-hyun's impeachment for proposing martial law to Yoon, and intended to file a criminal complaint against him. The party's chief spokesperson, Jo Seoung-lae, issued a statement: "The DPK will punish the unconstitutional and illegal emergency martial law of the Yoon administration ... We urge law enforcement agencies to immediately launch an investigation into the treason case that the entire nation is now aware of and bring the perpetrators to justice."[&#8202;](#ref-back-Jung-1)[\[32\]](#refnote-Jung) On 5 December, the police investigated Yoon and other key officials for alleged insurrection[&#8202;](#ref-back-248-0)[\[246\]](#refnote-248) in response to a case filed by minor opposition parties and 59 activists accusing them of treason.[&#8202;](#ref-back-249-0)[\[247\]](#refnote-249) Kim Yong-hyun resigned the same day and issued an apology.[&#8202;](#ref-back-250-0)[\[248\]](#refnote-250) He said "as defense minister, I feel deeply responsible and sorry for causing concern and confusion in regard to the martial law. All members of the armed forces involved in the implementation of the emergency martial law only did their duty at my direction. I take full responsibility for it." The DPK planned to appoint a permanent special counsel to investigate Yoon for treason[&#8202;](#ref-back-251-0)[\[249\]](#refnote-251) and considered filing charges against PPP Floor Leader Cho Kyung-ho, who urged PPP lawmakers to assemble at the party's headquarters rather than the Assembly.[&#8202;](#ref-back-252-0)[\[250\]](#refnote-252)

On 8 December, Kim Yong-hyun was arrested on suspicion of committing insurrection by advising Yoon to declare martial law and sending troops into the National Assembly to seize the legislature.[&#8202;](#ref-back-253-0)[\[251\]](#refnote-253)[&#8202;](#ref-back-254-0)[\[252\]](#refnote-254) The [Ministry of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Justice_(South_Korea) "Ministry of Justice (South Korea)") barred Kim from leaving the country following rumors that he booked a flight to flee overseas to avoid prosecution.[&#8202;](#ref-back-255-0)[\[253\]](#refnote-255)[&#8202;](#ref-back-kimflight-0)[\[254\]](#refnote-kimflight) He could potentially be sentenced to [life in prison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_in_prison "Life in prison"), or the [death penalty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_punishment_in_South_Korea "Capital punishment in South Korea") if found guilty.[&#8202;](#ref-back-hyunsuicide-0)[\[255\]](#refnote-hyunsuicide) On 9 December, the Ministry of Justice issued an overseas travel ban against Yoon following an investigation into allegations of rebellion linked to his brief imposition of martial law, marking the first instance of a sitting president facing such restrictions.[&#8202;](#ref-back-hyunsuicide-1)[\[255\]](#refnote-hyunsuicide)[&#8202;](#ref-back-258-0)[\[256\]](#refnote-258) That same day, Lee Jae Myung issued a statement exonerating lower-ranking soldiers who participated in the martial law exercises and expressed gratitude for their restraint, saying that they had been "exploited" by their commanders.[&#8202;](#ref-back-259-0)[\[257\]](#refnote-259) An overseas travel ban was placed on KNP Commissioner Cho Ji-ho, Seoul Metropolitan Police Agency Chief Kim Bong-sik and Mok Hyun-tae, head of the National Assembly Police Guards, as part of the martial law investigation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-260-0)[\[258\]](#refnote-260)[&#8202;](#ref-back-travelban-0)[\[259\]](#refnote-travelban)

On 10 December, the National Assembly passed a bill creating a permanent special counsel to investigate Yoon on charges of treason related to his martial law declaration. The motion passed with 210 MPs, including 23 PPP members, in favor after the PPP allowed its members to vote individually.[&#8202;](#ref-back-262-0)[\[260\]](#refnote-262) That same day, Army Maj. Gen. Moon Sang-ho, chief of the Defense Intelligence Command, was suspended over his role in the incursion into the National Election Commission's office in Gwacheon.[&#8202;](#ref-back-263-0)[\[261\]](#refnote-263) On 11 December, KNP Commissioner Cho Ji-ho and [Seoul Metropolitan Police Chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seoul_Metropolitan_Police_Agency "Seoul Metropolitan Police Agency") Kim Bong-sik were [arrested without a warrant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrest_without_warrant "Arrest without warrant") on charges of insurrection.[&#8202;](#ref-back-264-0)[\[262\]](#refnote-264) On 12 December, Park An-su was suspended as Army Chief of Staff.[&#8202;](#ref-back-265-0)[\[263\]](#refnote-265)

#### Arrest warrant and political standoff

Yoon Suk Yeol was summoned three times by the [Corruption Investigation Office for High-ranking Officials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_Investigation_Office_for_High-ranking_Officials "Corruption Investigation Office for High-ranking Officials") (CIO) for questioning on 18 December 25 December, and 29 December over his declaration of martial law. He ignored all three summonses.[&#8202;](#ref-back-266-0)[\[264\]](#refnote-266) In response, on 30 December, the CIO filed an arrest warrant for Yoon at the Seoul Western District Court.[&#8202;](#ref-back-267-0)[\[265\]](#refnote-267) On 31 December, the court issued the warrant,[&#8202;](#ref-back-268-0)[\[266\]](#refnote-268) which was valid until 6 January 2025.[&#8202;](#ref-back-269-0)[\[267\]](#refnote-269) After the impeachment, Yoon sequestered himself in the presidential residence.[&#8202;](#ref-back-270-0)[\[268\]](#refnote-270) On 1 January, he released a statement to his supporters pledging to "fight alongside you to the very end to protect this nation".[&#8202;](#ref-back-271-0)[\[269\]](#refnote-271) On 3 January, authorities tried to [serve the warrant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrest_of_Yoon_Suk_Yeol "Arrest of Yoon Suk Yeol") at the presidential residence but halted the attempt after being physically blocked by the Presidential Security Service.[&#8202;](#ref-back-272-0)[\[270\]](#refnote-272)[&#8202;](#ref-back-273-0)[\[271\]](#refnote-273) After the warrant expired on 6 January, the Seoul Western District Court extended the warrant's validity for one day.[&#8202;](#ref-back-274-0)[\[272\]](#refnote-274)

#### Police raids

On 10 December, police raided the Presidential Office, with investigators presenting a search warrant that specified Yoon as the suspect.[&#8202;](#ref-back-275-0)[\[273\]](#refnote-275)[&#8202;](#ref-back-276-0)[\[274\]](#refnote-276) The [Presidential Security Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Security_Service_(South_Korea) "Presidential Security Service (South Korea)") refused to cooperate, resulting in a "very limited" number of documents and materials being submitted by Yoon's office.[&#8202;](#ref-back-277-0)[\[275\]](#refnote-277) Raids were also conducted on the Defense Counterintelligence Command, the Army Special Warfare Command,[&#8202;](#ref-back-278-0)[\[276\]](#refnote-278) the National Police Agency, the Seoul Metropolitan Police Agency, and the National Assembly Security Service.[&#8202;](#ref-back-279-0)[\[277\]](#refnote-279)

On 12 December, police raided the Joint Chiefs of Staff headquarters adjacent to the presidential compound,[&#8202;](#ref-back-280-0)[\[278\]](#refnote-280) as well as the Capital Defense Command headquarters.[&#8202;](#ref-back-281-0)[\[279\]](#refnote-281) On 13 December, police raided the Gyeonggi Nambu Provincial Police headquarters to investigate the unit's role during martial law. Gyeonggi Nambu Provincial Police had dispatched police forces to the NEC after the martial law declaration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-282-0)[\[280\]](#refnote-282) On 17 December, a joint investigation team launched a raid on the offices of the Presidential Security Service.[&#8202;](#ref-back-283-0)[\[281\]](#refnote-283) The head of the Presidential Security Service, [Park Jong-jun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Park_Jong-jun "Park Jong-jun"), resigned on 10 January 2025. He was then summoned to the National Investigation Headquarters of the National Police Agency for questioning on charges of obstruction of official duties by preventing the arrest of Yoon.[&#8202;](#ref-back-284-0)[\[282\]](#refnote-284) Police raided a presidential safehouse in [Samcheong-dong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samcheong-dong "Samcheong-dong") believed to have hosted preparatory meetings for martial law and unsuccessfully tried to enter the Presidential Security Service.[&#8202;](#ref-back-285-0)[\[283\]](#refnote-285)

#### Suicide attempt of Kim Yong-hyun

On 10 December, at 23:52 [KST](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korea_Standard_Time "Korea Standard Time"), the former Defense Minister Kim Yong-hyun attempted suicide, using his clothing to try and [hang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanging "Hanging") himself in the bathroom of the detention facility where he was held in custody.[&#8202;](#ref-back-286-0)[\[284\]](#refnote-286)[&#8202;](#ref-back-287-0)[\[285\]](#refnote-287) Shin Yong-hae, the head of the Ministry of Justice's Correctional Service, said at a National Assembly plenary session: "A control room worker discovered Kim Yong-hyun attempting suicide by using string connecting his underwear and underwear pants in the bathroom of the waiting area before a warrant against him was to be issued. ... He gave up and came out when we immediately went in and opened the door. He is currently being housed in a protection facility and I have received reports that he is in good health."[&#8202;](#ref-back-kimhealth-0)[\[286\]](#refnote-kimhealth) Kim was indicted on insurrection charges on 27 December.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kim_Yong-hyun_indictment-5)[\[21\]](#refnote-Kim_Yong-hyun_indictment)

## Trials

### Impeachment trial

Main article: [Impeachment of Yoon Suk Yeol §Constitutional Court trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impeachment_of_Yoon_Suk_Yeol#Constitutional_Court_trial "Impeachment of Yoon Suk Yeol")

See also: [2025 Seoul Western District Court riot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Seoul_Western_District_Court_riot "2025 Seoul Western District Court riot")

On 14 January 2025, South Korea's [constitutional court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Korea "Constitutional Court of Korea") held the first hearing to determine if Yoon will be formally removed from office. The hearing was adjourned as it could not proceed without Yoon's presence.[&#8202;](#ref-back-289-0)[\[287\]](#refnote-289) On the morning of 15 January, [Yoon was arrested](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrest_of_Yoon_Suk_Yeol "Arrest of Yoon Suk Yeol") at his residence where he had been since the impeachment. Police used wire-cutters and ladders to enter Yoon's residence in order to bypass barricades and barbed wire fortifications. After his arrest Yoon was brought to the [Corruption Investigation Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_Investigation_Office_for_High-ranking_Officials "Corruption Investigation Office for High-ranking Officials") (CIO), where he agreed to an interrogation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-290-0)[\[288\]](#refnote-290)[&#8202;](#ref-back-DS-0)[\[289\]](#refnote-DS) Yoon is the country's first sitting president to be arrested.[&#8202;](#ref-back-DS-1)[\[289\]](#refnote-DS)[&#8202;](#ref-back-292-0)[\[290\]](#refnote-292)[&#8202;](#ref-back-293-0)[\[291\]](#refnote-293) On 18 January, supporters of Yoon clashed with police outside the Seoul Western District Court before [storming the courthouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Seoul_Western_District_Court_riot "2025 Seoul Western District Court riot") after his detention was extended.[&#8202;](#ref-back-294-0)[\[292\]](#refnote-294) On 21 January, Yoon attended the impeachment trial for the first time, during which he denied ordering soldiers to interfere with the proceedings of the National Assembly against the martial law declaration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-295-0)[\[293\]](#refnote-295)

On 23 January, the CIO recommended that Yoon be charged with "leading an insurrection and abuse of power".[&#8202;](#ref-back-296-0)[\[294\]](#refnote-296) On the same day, Yoon appeared at the impeachment trial again, with former Defense Minister Kim Yong-hyun present as a witness.[&#8202;](#ref-back-297-0)[\[295\]](#refnote-297) Kim denied allegations that Yoon had ordered the military to storm the National Assembly to prevent lawmakers from convening and passing a resolution nullifying martial law.[&#8202;](#ref-back-298-0)[\[296\]](#refnote-298) However, he admitted to recommending declaring martial law to Yoon and to writing a note to Finance Minister Choi Sang-mok, instructing the establishment of an emergency legislative body during martial law.[&#8202;](#ref-back-299-0)[\[297\]](#refnote-299) Alongside Yoon, Kim was also accused of ordering lawmakers to be dragged out of the parliament. Kim responded: "The situation was very chaotic, I thought someone might get crushed to death. So I said to pull them out for now to reduce the risk of serious harm. That's what I meant."[&#8202;](#ref-back-300-0)[\[298\]](#refnote-300)

Yoon was indicted on the insurrection charges on 26 January.[&#8202;](#ref-back-301-0)[\[299\]](#refnote-301) His case was assigned by the [Seoul Central District Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seoul_Central_District_Court "Seoul Central District Court") to its criminal collegiate division 25, which also handled martial law-related criminal charges against Kim Yong-hyun, Cho Ji-ho, and Kim Bong-sik.[&#8202;](#ref-back-302-0)[\[300\]](#refnote-302) On 20 February, Yoon's criminal trial began, making him the first incumbent president of South Korea to stand trial in a criminal case.[&#8202;](#ref-back-303-0)[\[301\]](#refnote-303) On 25 February, the last day of the impeachment trial, Yoon issued an apology on the last day of the impeachment trial,[&#8202;](#ref-back-304-0)[\[302\]](#refnote-304) and continued to defend his actions.[&#8202;](#ref-back-305-0)[\[303\]](#refnote-305)

### Verdict

[![see caption](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Judgment_of_Yoon_Suk_Yeol%27s_impeachment_in_4_April_2025.jpg/500px-Judgment_of_Yoon_Suk_Yeol%27s_impeachment_in_4_April_2025.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Judgment_of_Yoon_Suk_Yeol's_impeachment_in_4_April_2025.jpg) Constitutional Court Acting Chief Justice [Moon Hyung-bae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_Hyung-bae "Moon Hyung-bae") reads the verdict upholding Yoon's impeachment and removal from office, 4 April 2025.

[![Wikisource logo](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Wikisource-logo.svg/120px-Wikisource-logo.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Wikisource-logo.svg)

English [Wikisource](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikisource "Wikisource") has original text related to this article:

**[Case on Impeachment Trial of President (Yoon Suk-yeol) (Summary)](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/en:Case%20on%20Impeachment%20Trial%20of%20President%20(Yoon%20Suk-yeol)%20(Summary) "s:en:Case on Impeachment Trial of President (Yoon Suk-yeol) (Summary)")**

On 4 April, the Constitutional Court convened to announce its impeachment verdict. In a televised verdict read by acting Chief Justice [Moon Hyung-bae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_Hyung-bae "Moon Hyung-bae"), the judges unanimously decided in an 8–0 decision to remove Yoon from office, citing his betrayal of the [public trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_trust "Public trust") and serious violations of the law which failed to protect the Constitution. The Court removed Yoon from office on the basis of the following violations during martial law on 3–4 December:[&#8202;](#ref-back-306-0)[\[304\]](#refnote-306)[&#8202;](#ref-back-koreaherald-0)[\[305\]](#refnote-koreaherald)

- _**Missing substantive requirements for the declaration of emergency martial law**_

Political gridlock or suspicions of election fraud should have been resolved through political, institutional and judicial means. Yoon claimed that the declaration was intended as a "warning" or an "appeal to the public", but the Martial Law Act does not authorize declarations of martial law on those bases.

- _**Violation of procedural requirements set forth in the Constitution and the Martial Law Act for declaring emergency martial law**_

Yoon did not explain the specific details of the martial law to the martial law commander or other members, nor did he give the other members of the Cabinet the opportunity to state their opinions. Yoon declared martial law without obtaining the signatures of prime minister Han and relevant Cabinet members, failed to publicly announce when the martial law would be implemented, in which areas of the country martial law was being implemented, and who the martial law commander would be. Yoon also failed to notify the National Assembly of martial law implementation without delay.

- _**Interference with the National Assembly's authority to overturn martial law**_

Yoon deployed the military and police to block the Assembly members from entering the National Assembly and ordered them dragged out. These commands violated the constitutional provision that grants the Assembly the right to demand the lifting of martial law, lawmakers' rights to deliberate and vote, and their immunity from arrest.

- _**Violation of citizens' freedom to actively support any political party**_

Yoon directly requested NIS first deputy director [Hong Jang-won](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Jang-won "Hong Jang-won") to track the locations of his political opponents for potential arrests.

- _**Infringement on military neutrality in civil affairs and the commander-in-chief's duties under the Constitution**_

Deploying military troops for political purposes, to block the functions and exercise of power by the National Assembly, led soldiers into direct conflict with civilians.

- _**Infringement on Constitutional guarantees of the political party system, representative democracy and separated powers**_

The martial law decree prohibited the activities of the National Assembly, regional assemblies and political parties.

- _**Violation of basic political rights, especially the rights to collective action and freedom of occupation**_

Yoon had violating provisions of the Constitution and Martial Law Act that stipulate the requirements for restricting basic rights under martial law and need for [warrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warrant_(law) "Warrant (law)").

- _**Unauthorized search and seizure without a warrant at the National Election Commission and infringement of its independence**_

Yoon's order to the defense minister Kim to mobilize troops to inspect the National Election Commission's computer systems, raid the NEC, confiscate of on-duty NEC staff members' phones, and film NEC computer systems infringed the independence of the NEC. Search and seizure at the NEC without a warrant violated the warrant requirement. The NEC had already taken measures to prevent election fraud before the last National Assembly election, contrary to Yoon's claims.

- _**Violation of judicial independence**_

Tracking the locations of former chief justice [Kim Myeong-su](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Myeong-su "Kim Myeong-su") and former justices of the Supreme Court such as ex-justice Kwon Soon-il (former chair of the NEC) for potential arrest created pressure on current judges, suggesting they could be subject to arrest by the executive branch at any time.

Upon the verdict's delivery at 11:22a.m. KST, Yoon ceased to be president of South Korea, triggering a presidential election due to take place within 60 days. This paved the way for Yoon to be charged with other crimes in addition to insurrection as he no longer had presidential immunity.

### Insurrection trial

Yoon's trial on charges of insurrection began on 14 April.[&#8202;](#ref-back-308-0)[\[306\]](#refnote-308) He attended the fifth hearing of the trial on 26 May, and he declined to respond to questions from the press.[&#8202;](#ref-back-309-0)[\[307\]](#refnote-309) On 6 July 2025, South Korean prosecutors filed a request to detain Yoon a day after he appeared before them for questioning over his declaration of martial law the previous year.[&#8202;](#ref-back-310-0)[\[308\]](#refnote-310) He would then once again be arrested on 10 July 2025[&#8202;](#ref-back-311-0)[\[309\]](#refnote-311) and indicted for abuse of power on 19 July.[&#8202;](#ref-back-312-0)[\[310\]](#refnote-312) On 18 July 2025, the Seoul District Court denied Yoon's request to be released from prison,[&#8202;](#ref-back-313-0)[\[311\]](#refnote-313) with the additional indictment then requiring for him to remain in prison for up to six months.[&#8202;](#ref-back-314-0)[\[312\]](#refnote-314)

If found guilty, Yoon potentially faced the [death penalty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_punishment_in_South_Korea "Capital punishment in South Korea") or [life imprisonment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_imprisonment "Life imprisonment"), although there has been a moratorium on executions in South Korea since 1997.[&#8202;](#ref-back-315-0)[\[313\]](#refnote-315) On 13 January 2026, the prosecution sought the death penalty for Yoon,[&#8202;](#ref-back-n260113-0)[\[314\]](#refnote-n260113) stating that his actions constituted an "unprecedented and grave act of constitutional destruction" and that there were no [mitigating circumstances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitigating_factor "Mitigating factor").[&#8202;](#ref-back-n260113-1)[\[314\]](#refnote-n260113)[&#8202;](#ref-back-317-0)[\[315\]](#refnote-317) On 19 February 2026, the court ruled that Yoon had orchestrated an insurrection and engaged in actions aimed at undermining the nation's constitutional system, sentencing him to life imprisonment.[&#8202;](#ref-back-sentencing1-1)[\[7\]](#refnote-sentencing1)

#### Special counsel investigation

On 4 June 2025, [Lee Jae Myung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Jae_Myung "Lee Jae Myung") of the Democratic Party took office as president after winning the [2025 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_South_Korean_presidential_election "2025 South Korean presidential election").[&#8202;](#ref-back-318-0)[\[316\]](#refnote-318) On 5 June, the DPK-controlled National Assembly passed a bill to set up a special counsel investigation into Yoon's martial law, after being vetoed multiple times by the previous acting presidents.[&#8202;](#ref-back-319-0)[\[317\]](#refnote-319) The bill mandated a permanent special counsel investigation against Yoon, and would look into 11 different charges tied to his martial law attempt, including insurrection and mutiny. President Lee's government promulgated and enacted the bills on 10 June.

Cho Eun-suk, former chief of the Seoul High Prosecutors Office and former acting chief of the Board of Audit and Inspection, was appointed as the special counsel by President Lee.[&#8202;](#ref-back-320-0)[\[318\]](#refnote-320) The investigation team was made up of 60 prosecutors.[&#8202;](#ref-back-321-0)[\[319\]](#refnote-321) This meant that the concurrent probes by the police, prosecutors and CIO would be merged into the special counsel investigation. The ongoing criminal cases against Yoon were transferred to the special prosecutor on 19 June.[&#8202;](#ref-back-322-0)[\[320\]](#refnote-322)

Among the first acts of the special counsel investigation was to extend Kim Yong-hyun's detention by indicting him with additional charges of obstruction of justice and abetting the destruction of evidence just as he was set to be released. Military prosecutors filed new charges against Yeo In-hyung and Moon Sang-ho, with further investigations into Roh Sang-won. The special counsel team also began investigating allegations that two Defense Intelligence Command officers were caught by Mongolia's intelligence agency trying to contact the North Korean Embassy in Mongolia, ten days before the imposition of martial law, as a key clue pointing to an attempt to bait North Korea into a skirmish.[&#8202;](#ref-back-323-0)[\[321\]](#refnote-323)

## Legal proceedings, criminal charges and sentences

On 15 December 2025, former Commander of the Defense Intelligence Command Roh Sang-won was found guilty of violating the Personal Information Protection Act by requesting and receiving personal details such as rank, origin, and commission year of 46 Intelligence Command personnel from Moon Sang-ho, then commander of the Information Command, to plan a non-official investigative body to probe alleged election fraud between September and December 2024. Roh was sentenced to 2 years in prison. He was ordered to pay 24.9 million won after he was found guilty of receiving 20,000,000 Korean won in cash and 6,000,000 Korean won worth of department store gift certificates from former head of the Central News Unit at the Information Command Kim Bong-gyu, and former commander of the 2nd Armored Brigade Koo Sam-hoe, under the pretext of helping their promotions between August and October 2024.[&#8202;](#ref-back-324-0)[\[322\]](#refnote-324)[&#8202;](#ref-back-325-0)[\[323\]](#refnote-325) On 18 December, the Constitutional Court upheld the National Assembly's impeachment of Police Commissioner Cho Ji-ho and removed him from office by a unanimous 9–0 decision, on grounds of grave violation of the principles of representative democracy and separation of powers, by following illegal orders by Yoon to block lawmakers from entering the National Assembly and deploying police officers to the National Election Commission headquarters which interfered with the commission's duties and its exercise of authority.[&#8202;](#ref-back-326-0)[\[324\]](#refnote-326)

On 16 January 2026, Yoon Suk Yeol was found guilty on charges of infringement of ministers' constitutional right to deliberate on the imposition of martial law, obstruction of arrest, abuse of power, falsification and unlawful destruction of official documents relating to the martial law declaration, and masterminding an insurrection, and was sentenced to life in prison plus 12 years.[&#8202;](#ref-back-327-0)[\[325\]](#refnote-327)[&#8202;](#ref-back-328-0)[\[326\]](#refnote-328) On 21 January, former Prime Minister Han Duck-soo was convicted of falsifying documents, destroying presidential records, committing [perjury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perjury "Perjury") during Yoon's impeachment proceedings, and fabricating the appearance of a legitimate cabinet meeting to approve an unconstitutional decree, and was sentenced to 23 years in prison.[&#8202;](#ref-back-329-0)[\[327\]](#refnote-329)[&#8202;](#ref-back-330-0)[\[328\]](#refnote-330) The sentence was reduced to 15 years imprisonment on appeal while upholding the guilty verdict on 7 May 2026.[&#8202;](#ref-back-331-0)[\[329\]](#refnote-331) On 12 February, former Interior and Safety Minister [Lee Sang-min](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Sang-min_(lawyer) "Lee Sang-min (lawyer)") was convicted of ordering to cut electricity and water to media outlets critical of Yoon's policies, and falsely testifying during Yoon's impeachment trial that he had never relayed these orders, and sentenced to 9 years in prison.[&#8202;](#ref-back-AP-0)[\[330\]](#refnote-AP)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Byun-0)[\[331\]](#refnote-Byun) He was found not guilty on abuse of power charges.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Byun-1)[\[331\]](#refnote-Byun)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Bahk-0)[\[332\]](#refnote-Bahk)[&#8202;](#ref-back-appeal-0)[\[333\]](#refnote-appeal)

On 19 February 2026, the main insurrection trial concluded.[&#8202;](#ref-back-336-0)[\[334\]](#refnote-336)[&#8202;](#ref-back-337-0)[\[335\]](#refnote-337) Former Defense Minister Kim Yong-hyun was found guilty of participating and planning the insurrection, destruction of evidence, and obstruction of official duties by deception, and was sentenced to 30 years in prison. Former Commissioner General of the Korea National Police Agency, Cho Ji-ho and former Seoul Metropolitan Police Agency chief Kim Bong-sik were found guilty of playing key roles in the insurrection by deploying police units to the National Assembly, and were sentenced to 12 years and 10 years in prison, respectively. Former Commander of the Defense Intelligence Command Noh Sang-won was found guilty of playing a key role in the insurrection, and was sentenced to 18 years in prison. Seoul Metropolitan Police Agency National Assembly Security Unit Commander Mok Hyun-tae was found guilty of playing a key role in the insurrection and was sentenced to 3 years in prison. Former Commander of the Third Field Army Command's Military Police Unit Kim Yong-gun and former director of investigation planning and coordination at the Korean National Police Agency Yoon Seung-young were acquitted after being found not guilty of participating in insurrection.[&#8202;](#ref-back-338-0)[\[336\]](#refnote-338) Former NIS Director [Cho Tae-yong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cho_Tae-yong "Cho Tae-yong") was found guilty of perjury and destruction of evidence, was sentenced to 18 months in prison.[&#8202;](#ref-back-339-0)[\[337\]](#refnote-339)

## Opinion polling

Opinion polling carried out by Realmeter on 4 December found that 73.4% of respondents supported Yoon's impeachment while 24% opposed it, and 70% believed that Yoon's actions constituted treason while 25% believed otherwise.[&#8202;](#ref-back-340-0)[\[338\]](#refnote-340) A poll by [Gallup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup,_Inc. "Gallup, Inc.") conducted from 3 to 5 December found that Yoon's approval rating had fallen by six percentage points to 13% since the martial law declaration, while the PPP's approval rating had fallen by five percentage points to 27%. The DPK's approval rating had increased by four percentage points to 37%.[&#8202;](#ref-back-341-0)[\[339\]](#refnote-341)[&#8202;](#ref-back-342-0)[\[340\]](#refnote-342)

A Realmeter poll, released on 12 December, found 74.8% of respondents supported either Yoon's immediate resignation or impeachment, while 16.2% supported the PPP's proposal of Yoon's orderly resignation. 26.2% of respondents experienced trauma caused by the martial law declaration and recovered, and 40.0% continued to experience trauma.[&#8202;](#ref-back-343-0)[\[341\]](#refnote-343) A Gallup poll released on 13 December found that Yoon's approval rating had fallen further to 11%, with 49% of respondents citing his declaration of martial law as a reason to assess him negatively. 75% of respondents supported Yoon's impeachment, while 21% opposed. The PPP's approval rating had fallen by three percentage points to 24%, while the DPK's approval rating had increased by three percentage points to 40%.[&#8202;](#ref-back-344-0)[\[342\]](#refnote-344) The poll found Speaker Woo Won-shik emerging as the most trusted politician in South Korea for his actions during martial law and the subsequent impeachment, with a rating of 56%.[&#8202;](#ref-back-345-0)[\[343\]](#refnote-345)

On 31 December, a poll conducted by _[The Korea Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Korea_Times "The Korea Times")_ and Hankook Research found 98% of DPK supporters believed that Yoon committed treason in declaring martial law, while 81% of PPP supporters believed otherwise, with only 12% agreeing.[&#8202;](#ref-back-346-0)[\[344\]](#refnote-346) 56% of respondents were in favor of amending the constitutional provisions on presidential power, while 39% were opposed. 45% wanted constitutional reforms to address the shortcomings of the single, five-year term system, while 35% wanted to limit presidential powers to ensure better checks and balances, of which 64% specifically cited the presidential veto power. Another 17% wanted to overhaul the presidential election system.[&#8202;](#ref-back-347-0)[\[345\]](#refnote-347)

### Impact on South Korean military personnel

Concerns have been raised about morale and combat readiness in the Republic of Korea Armed Forces, following reported distrust in their leaders by rank-and-file troops after being sent to execute orders relating to the martial law declaration, and suspensions of senior military officials implicated or under investigation for their roles in the martial law declaration, potentially leading to leadership vacuums and low morale.[&#8202;](#ref-back-348-0)[\[346\]](#refnote-348) On 18 December, the Ministry of National Defense found "at least several dozen soldiers" were at high risk of developing [post-traumatic stress disorder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-traumatic_stress_disorder "Post-traumatic stress disorder") (PTSD) and required special care, after conducting psychological evaluations on all personnel dispatched during the martial law incident. The Ministry also reported that many were likely reluctant to seek counseling due to fear of being identified for their roles in the martial law operations. In response, the ministry introduced a civilian psychological counseling program to ensure anonymous support for affected troops.[&#8202;](#ref-back-349-0)[\[347\]](#refnote-349) A report by [JTBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JTBC "JTBC") on 27 February 2025 found that the ministry deemed 71 soldiers as in need of treatment for mental health issues following a psychological evaluation, with two of them in a dangerous category.[&#8202;](#ref-back-350-0)[\[348\]](#refnote-350)

## Reception and analysis

The students and staff of Chungam High School, Yoon Suk Yeol's alma mater, received public backlash by citizens over the martial law declaration and the Chungam Faction. In return, the school announced that students were not mandated to wear their uniforms to school until graduation in an attempt to protect them from harassment.[&#8202;](#ref-back-351-0)[\[349\]](#refnote-351) They also confirmed they had no association with both the declaration and the faction. Chungam High School Foundation Chairwoman Yoon Myung-hwa called Yoon Suk Yeol and Kim Yong-hyun "shameful graduates" in a social media post,[&#8202;](#ref-back-koreatimes.co.kr-1)[\[218\]](#refnote-koreatimes.co.kr) and said in another post: "The school's office is flooded with complaint calls all day, and even school bus drivers report being harassed by citizens passing by. There's even a petition to change the school's name. The school's reputation and the country's honor are tarnished. What kind of mental anguish must our students be enduring?"\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] Leif-Eric Easley, a professor of international studies at [Ewha Womans University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ewha_Womans_University "Ewha Womans University") in Seoul, compared it to the [January 6 United States Capitol attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_6_United_States_Capitol_attack "January 6 United States Capitol attack") and the [8 January Brasília attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8_January_Bras%C3%ADlia_attacks "8 January Brasília attacks"), saying the effects of the declaration of martial law on South Korean politics and its reputation would be far worse than what happened in the United States.[&#8202;](#ref-back-352-0)[\[350\]](#refnote-352)

Students at Myungil Girls' High School, Kim Keon-hee's alma mater, put up posters decrying Kim in response to the martial law order. Youngshik Bong, an adviser to the [Ministry of Unification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Unification "Ministry of Unification") and visiting professor at [Yonsei University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yonsei_University "Yonsei University"), said that declaring martial law should be reserved for the most serious situations, such as war. He added that this will backfire on the president as "his impeachment is really in the cards now".[&#8202;](#ref-back-353-0)[\[351\]](#refnote-353) Park Sung-min, a political consultant in Seoul, said "I don't know what his end goal was here, because I think this was political suicide ... It seems clear that President Yoon is now more likely to step down in the middle of his term rather than seeing his term through to completion".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Ye-2)[\[62\]](#refnote-Ye) Shim Young-sub, an [adjunct professor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adjunct_professor "Adjunct professor") of media video promotion at [Kyung Hee Cyber University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyung_Hee_Cyber_University "Kyung Hee Cyber University"), noted that the martial law decree only mentioned regulations on legacy media but did not include the internet. Shim said attempting to suppress free speech "using a martial law document reminiscent of the 1980s ... was a pipe dream".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Hankyoreh_internet_role-0)[\[352\]](#refnote-Hankyoreh_internet_role) Yang Sang-hoon, editor-in-chief of the conservative _[The Chosun Ilbo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Chosun_Ilbo "The Chosun Ilbo")_ newspaper, called Yoon's martial law the "most foolish self-destructive incident in the history of Korean presidents" and apologized for doubting earlier predictions that Yoon would make such a move.[&#8202;](#ref-back-355-0)[\[353\]](#refnote-355)

During a press conference in Stockholm on 6 December before her Nobel lecture, [2024 Nobel Prize in Literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Nobel_Prize_in_Literature "2024 Nobel Prize in Literature") laureate South Korea's [Han Kang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_Kang "Han Kang"), who wrote the novel _[Human Acts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Acts "Human Acts")_ inspired by the 1980 [Gwangju Uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwangju_Uprising "Gwangju Uprising"), called the martial law declaration a shock, but described sensing the "truthfulness and courage" of "unarmed citizens attempting to stop armed soldiers ... young police and soldiers who moved reluctantly, as if sensing some inner conflict ... striving to do the least amount of harm as possible".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Hankyoreh_internet_role-1)[\[352\]](#refnote-Hankyoreh_internet_role)[&#8202;](#ref-back-356-0)[\[354\]](#refnote-356) The declaration of martial law triggered memories in older Koreans of past military dictatorships and the authoritarian era, while younger Koreans, educated and exposed to elders sharing their experiences about abuses under past military rule, reacted with alarm and disbelief while recalling lessons about torture, imprisoned opposition leaders and deadly crackdowns on pro-democracy protests in the past. Unlike past military coups which had the endorsement of the United States, this was mainly a domestic political issue that was handled swiftly and decisively by South Korean citizens without external interference. Combined with troops refusing to abide by unjust orders, these were cited as reasons to believe that South Korean democracy and society has evolved in the last 4 decades to the point that a return to military rule or dictatorship would not be accepted.[&#8202;](#ref-back-echoes-1)[\[115\]](#refnote-echoes)

On 12 December, 510 [psychiatrists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychiatrist "Psychiatrist") issued a statement that "since Dec. 3, the whole nation has been suffering psychologically". They said in the statement "many people who remember the history of military dictatorship and state violence were forced to relive the trauma and experience a serious level of fear".[&#8202;](#ref-back-357-0)[\[355\]](#refnote-357) In the days following the botched martial law order, the 2023 film _[12.12: The Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/12.12:_The_Day "12.12: The Day")_, a depiction of [the coup organized by Chun Doo-hwan in 1979](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coup_d'%C3%A9tat_of_December_Twelfth "Coup d'état of December Twelfth") became the no. 1 South Korean film on the streaming platform [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix "Netflix"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-358-0)[\[356\]](#refnote-358) while memes appeared online of main character Chun Doo-kwang's (based on Chun Doo-hwan) face replaced with President Yoon, and posts comparing scenes from the film with the footage of the recent events. There were calls for a theatrical re-release of the film.[&#8202;](#ref-back-359-0)[\[357\]](#refnote-359) A letter signed by 3,000 members of South Korea's film industry, including _[Parasite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parasite_(2019_film) "Parasite (2019 film)")_ director [Bong Joon-ho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bong_Joon-ho "Bong Joon-ho"), said the martial law declaration threatened to send the [Korean wave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_wave "Korean wave") "into the abyss".[&#8202;](#ref-back-360-0)[\[358\]](#refnote-360)

### International analysis

In the United States, _[Foreign Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Policy "Foreign Policy")_ magazine,[&#8202;](#ref-back-Foreign_Policy_magazine_self-coup-2)[\[48\]](#refnote-Foreign_Policy_magazine_self-coup) the [Associated Press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Associated_Press "Associated Press"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-361-0)[\[359\]](#refnote-361) political scientists [Sidney Tarrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sidney_Tarrow "Sidney Tarrow")[&#8202;](#ref-back-362-0)[\[360\]](#refnote-362) and Benjamin Engel,[&#8202;](#ref-back-363-0)[\[361\]](#refnote-363) and coup historians Joe Wright and John J. Chin[&#8202;](#ref-back-theconversation.com-1)[\[3\]](#refnote-theconversation.com) described the events as an attempted [self-coup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-coup "Self-coup"). The impeachment motion in the National Assembly also described Yoon's actions as a self-coup.[&#8202;](#ref-back-364-0)[\[362\]](#refnote-364) _[The New Yorker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_Yorker "The New Yorker")_ characterized it as "a coup, almost" and an "intended coup".[&#8202;](#ref-back-365-0)[\[363\]](#refnote-365) In the United Kingdom, _[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist "The Economist")_ described the event as an attempted coup.[&#8202;](#ref-back-366-0)[\[364\]](#refnote-366) The [Economist Intelligence Unit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economist_Intelligence_Unit "Economist Intelligence Unit") cited the declaration as its reason to downgrade South Korea from a "full democracy" to a "flawed democracy" in its 2024 [Democracy Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy_Index "Democracy Index").[&#8202;](#ref-back-367-0)[\[365\]](#refnote-367) [BBC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_News "BBC News") reported one Korean resident comparing it to the [2021 Myanmar coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Myanmar_coup_d'%C3%A9tat "2021 Myanmar coup d'état").[&#8202;](#ref-back-368-0)[\[366\]](#refnote-368)

[Reporters Without Borders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reporters_Without_Borders "Reporters Without Borders") criticized the declaration of martial law and its provisions allowing for military control of the press, noting that it would have led to the South Korean presidency gaining "total control over the media for the first time since the country's democratization".[&#8202;](#ref-back-369-0)[\[367\]](#refnote-369) The martial law declaration was cited by [Freedom House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_House "Freedom House") in its decision to give South Korea a score of 81 out of 100 in its [Freedom in the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_in_the_World "Freedom in the World") 2025 index, a reduction of two points from the previous year. The organization said that the "move highlighted one of the biggest threats faced by democracies around the world: elected leaders who attack democratic institutions".[&#8202;](#ref-back-370-0)[\[368\]](#refnote-370)

### International diplomatic statements

Multiple countries issued advisories urging caution, advising their citizens in South Korea to be vigilant and avoid public demonstrations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-371-0)[\[369\]](#refnote-371) The [White House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_Office_of_the_President_of_the_United_States "Executive Office of the President of the United States") and the [United States Department of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_State "United States Department of State") said they were not given notice ahead of time of Yoon's intention to declare martial law,[&#8202;](#ref-back-372-0)[\[370\]](#refnote-372) while South Korean Foreign Minister Cho Tae-yul refused to take calls from US ambassador [Philip Goldberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip_Goldberg "Philip Goldberg") for the duration of martial law.[&#8202;](#ref-back-373-0)[\[371\]](#refnote-373) State Department spokesperson [Vedant Patel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vedant_Patel "Vedant Patel") expressed "grave concern" for the ongoing developments while reiterating the United States' "iron-clad" alliance with South Korea.[&#8202;](#ref-back-374-0)[\[372\]](#refnote-374) Deputy Secretary of State [Kurt M. Campbell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurt_M._Campbell "Kurt M. Campbell") called Yoon's decision to impose martial law "badly-misjudged".[&#8202;](#ref-back-375-0)[\[373\]](#refnote-375) The White House later expressed relief at the lifting of martial law,[&#8202;](#ref-back-376-0)[\[374\]](#refnote-376) as did United Nations Secretary-General [Antonio Guterres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Guterres "Antonio Guterres").[&#8202;](#ref-back-377-0)[\[375\]](#refnote-377)

Italian Foreign Minister [Antonio Tajani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Tajani "Antonio Tajani") said that [NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO "NATO") warned North Korea not to take advantage of the situation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-378-0)[\[376\]](#refnote-378) The United States [Department of Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Defense "United States Department of Defense") said it had not received a request for military assistance from South Korea during the declaration of martial law,[&#8202;](#ref-back-379-0)[\[377\]](#refnote-379) adding that there was no force posture change in the [United States Forces Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Forces_Korea "United States Forces Korea") (USFK).[&#8202;](#ref-back-380-0)[\[378\]](#refnote-380) USFK Commander General [Paul LaCamera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_LaCamera "Paul LaCamera") urged personnel and their families to "exercise individual vigilance".[&#8202;](#ref-back-381-0)[\[379\]](#refnote-381)

[Kyrgyzstan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyrgyzstan "Kyrgyzstan")'s President [Sadyr Japarov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sadyr_Japarov "Sadyr Japarov") was on a state visit in South Korea when martial law was declared.[&#8202;](#ref-back-382-0)[\[380\]](#refnote-382) The Kyrgyz government issued a statement confirming the safety of the president and his delegation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-383-0)[\[381\]](#refnote-383) Japarov concluded the state visit and returned to Kyrgyzstan on 4 December.[&#8202;](#ref-back-384-0)[\[382\]](#refnote-384) Swedish Prime Minister [Ulf Kristersson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulf_Kristersson "Ulf Kristersson") cancelled his scheduled visit to South Korea and summit with Yoon.[&#8202;](#ref-back-385-0)[\[383\]](#refnote-385) Meetings of the Nuclear Consultative Group and related military exercises were also postponed indefinitely by the United States,[&#8202;](#ref-back-386-0)[\[384\]](#refnote-386) along with a visit by Defense Secretary [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin "Lloyd Austin").[&#8202;](#ref-back-387-0)[\[385\]](#refnote-387) Japanese Defense Minister [Gen Nakatani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gen_Nakatani "Gen Nakatani") postponed a scheduled visit in late December.[&#8202;](#ref-back-388-0)[\[386\]](#refnote-388) Kazakhstan cancelled defense meetings with its South Korean counterparts and a visit by Defense Minister [Ruslan Jaqsylyqov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruslan_Jaqsylyqov "Ruslan Jaqsylyqov") on 5 December, while a visit by Japanese MPs from the [Japan-Korea Parliamentarians' Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan-Korea_Parliamentarians'_Union "Japan-Korea Parliamentarians' Union") led by former Prime Minister [Yoshihide Suga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoshihide_Suga "Yoshihide Suga") was cancelled. South Korean Foreign Minister [Cho Tae-yul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cho_Tae-yul "Cho Tae-yul") cancelled his scheduled address at the World Emerging Security Forum in Seoul on 5 December, while Deputy Foreign Ministers [Kim Hong-kyun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Hong-kyun "Kim Hong-kyun") and Kang In-sun respectively cut short and cancelled their attendance at diplomatic meetings in Europe and the United Arab Emirates. The Ministry of Foreign Affairs issued diplomatic notes to all embassies inside South Korea emphasizing the stabilization of the domestic situation following martial law.[&#8202;](#ref-back-389-0)[\[387\]](#refnote-389)[&#8202;](#ref-back-390-0)[\[388\]](#refnote-390)

#### In Taiwan

In [Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan "Taiwan"), the [Democratic Progressive Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Progressive_Party "Democratic Progressive Party") Legislative Caucus posted an article on [Threads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Threads_(social_network) "Threads (social network)"), claiming that the declaration of martial law was a legitimate effort to protect free constitutional democracy in South Korea and criticizing Taiwanese opposition parties for obstructing national security proposals, allegedly "unconstitutionally expand\[&#8202;](#ref-back-Thompson-0)[ing\]" their powers, and reducing the defense budget.[\[389\]](#refnote-Thompson)[&#8202;](#ref-back-392-0)[\[390\]](#refnote-392) While the post was deleted shortly afterwards, it sparked criticism from [major opposition parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_political_parties_in_Taiwan "List of political parties in Taiwan"), including the [Kuomintang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuomintang "Kuomintang") and the [Taiwan People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_People's_Party "Taiwan People's Party"), who interpreted it as a threat to take similar measures in Taiwan.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Thompson-1)[\[389\]](#refnote-Thompson)[&#8202;](#ref-back-393-0)[\[391\]](#refnote-393)

### Coverage in North Korea

Despite having previously reported negatively on the Yoon administration on a regular basis, the state-controlled North Korean press initially refrained from reporting on the martial law declaration and its aftermath. Some South Korean analysts believed this was to avoid provoking cross-border tensions and encouraging resistance against the North Korean government.[&#8202;](#ref-back-northyoon-0)[\[392\]](#refnote-northyoon)[&#8202;](#ref-back-395-0)[\[393\]](#refnote-395) On 11 December 2024, North Korean state media released its first statements on the martial law declaration through an article published in the newspaper _[Rodong Sinmun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodong_Sinmun "Rodong Sinmun")_,[&#8202;](#ref-back-NK-0)[\[394\]](#refnote-NK) describing it as an "insane act" that was "akin to the [coup d'état of the decades-ago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coup_d'%C3%A9tat_of_May_Seventeenth "Coup d'état of May Seventeenth") military dictatorship era".[&#8202;](#ref-back-ML-0)[\[395\]](#refnote-ML) It also described the incident as having "revealed the weakness in South Korean society" and that it hinted at the end of Yoon's political career. The newspaper also published images of anti-Yoon protests in Seoul.[&#8202;](#ref-back-ML-1)[\[395\]](#refnote-ML)[&#8202;](#ref-back-NK-1)[\[394\]](#refnote-NK)[&#8202;](#ref-back-northyoon-1)[\[392\]](#refnote-northyoon) On the same day, North Korea's state TV described the incident as "chaos" and called the South a "fascist dictatorship".[&#8202;](#ref-back-398-0)[\[396\]](#refnote-398) On 3 January 2025, North Korean state media said that South Korea was in "political chaos" amid ongoing attempts to arrest Yoon.[&#8202;](#ref-back-399-0)[\[397\]](#refnote-399)

## Commemoration

On 17 July 2025, a monument was unveiled in front of the National Assembly building commemorating the vote that overturned martial law and the protests that occurred against the declaration. It contains an engraving: "The National Assembly of the Republic of Korea – the final bastion of democracy".[&#8202;](#ref-back-400-0)[\[398\]](#refnote-400)
